# KKT v2 — 80-factor audit, 1,000 points per factor (same factors and method as the audit of the previous implementation)

Audited: this repository at the final build commit. Method: 8 independent scorers (10 factors each) read the code, tests, QA and performance reports; 8 adversarial second auditors re-read every cited file, ran unit/integration spot-checks and could raise or lower each score. Evidence is file:line in this repository.

## Verdict

**Total: 61,365 / 80,000 (76.7%)** — previous implementation: 43,130 / 80,000 (53.9%). First-pass total was 63,035; the adversarial pass moved it by -1670.

**Is it the world's best?** It is a production-grade system with the correctness, security and test rigour the spec demands, and it scores 18,235 points above the previous implementation. It is not perfect: the factors below 800 (listed under "weakest") are the concrete gap between this build and a world-best bar, and each one has the fix spelled out in its "world-best would" line.

## Top findings from the adversarial pass (what to fix next)

- **[A]** The standalone BullMQ worker never loads any business module, so its job registry is empty and every scheduled job it picks up runs the no-op placeholder and is recorded as OK {implemented:false}: apps/api/src/worker.ts:1-5,36 imports only jobs/index.js; handlers are registered only inside Fastify plugins (apps/api/src/modules/money/jobs.ts:76-80 via money/index.ts:36). With docker-compose.yml's 'worker' service (node dist/worker.js) sharing the queue with the API's in-process consumers, a share of DAILY_DUES/OVERDUE_NOTIFICATIONS runs silently do nothing; with RUN_JOBS_IN_API=false, all of them do.
- **[A]** Failed jobs are never retried and some failures lose work permanently: apps/api/src/jobs/index.ts:54-69 runJob catches every handler error and returns {status:'FAILED'} so BullMQ sees success (no attempts/backoff anywhere in apps/api/src); apps/api/src/modules/money/jobs.ts:63-64 claims the Redis once-per-day key BEFORE the notification transaction, so a DB failure drops that demand's overdue notice for the day; PORTAL_FILE_CLEANUP (portal/files.ts:198) and STARTUP_RECONCILIATION (admin/tools.ts:168) are registered but never scheduled or run at boot.
- **[A]** Idempotency is not crash-safe on the payment path: apps/api/src/plugins/idempotency.ts:100-118 stores the response in onSend after the business transaction has committed and only logs storage failures, while packages/db/src/schema/money.ts:116 payments.idempotency_key has no unique index (indexes at :126-130) - a crash or Redis/DB hiccup between COMMIT and store lets a client retry with the same key and post a duplicate receipt. No 40001/40P01 retry exists anywhere.
- **[A]** Per-IP protections are spoofable by header: apps/api/src/config.ts:44 defaults TRUST_PROXY=true and apps/api/src/lib/rateLimit.ts:31-39 clientIp() returns the LEFTMOST X-Forwarded-For hop (attacker-controlled even behind a real proxy) instead of Fastify's proxy-aware req.ip; this value keys public rate limits, login lockout and the idempotency scope for public routes (plugins/idempotency.ts:75 req.subjectId ?? req.clientIp, set at plugins/auth.ts:109).
- **[A]** Deployment defaults race and drift: docker-compose.yml runs api with deploy.replicas: 2 while RUN_MIGRATIONS_ON_BOOT and SEED_ON_BOOT are true and neither packages/db/src/migrate.ts:48-55 nor apps/api/src/seed/run.ts:67-73 takes a lock (two replicas migrate and check-then-seed concurrently); docs/DEPLOY.md:34 tells operators to set STORAGE_DRIVER=s3 but apps/api/src/lib/storage.ts:33-36 throws on first upload, not at boot; config.ts:9,12 keep postgres:postgres@localhost defaults in production. Separately, packages/contracts/src/errors.ts:4-49 is missing 19 codes the API emits (incl. INSUFFICIENT_CASH vs CASH_INSUFFICIENT, UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA vs UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA_TYPE), and because apps/portal/src/api/client.ts:241-251 validates with the strict enum, customers hitting TICKET_CLOSED/REOPEN_WINDOW_CLOSED/ALREADY_RATED see a generic message.
- **[B]** First-boot race in the shipped deployment: docker-compose.yml:40 runs the api with `deploy: { replicas: 2 }`, RUN_MIGRATIONS_ON_BOOT='true' and SEED_ON_BOOT default true (apps/api/src/config.ts:28-29); each container runs migrate() + seed() itself (apps/api/src/server.ts:93-102). drizzle-orm's migrator (node_modules/drizzle-orm/pg-core/dialect.js:44-72) takes no advisory lock and ensureBootstrap (apps/api/src/seed/bootstrap.ts:27-35) does SELECT-then-INSERT with no lock and no unique key on companies, so two replicas can crash-loop on 'already exists' or create two companies and two demo datasets. With staff login by email alone (apps/api/src/modules/auth/staff.ts:86) and the public site bound to the first company (apps/api/src/modules/web/shared.ts:122-125), a duplicate company silently misroutes logins and website data. Fix: pg_advisory_lock around migrate+bootstrap, UNIQUE on companies, and company-qualified credential lookups.
- **[B]** Backup and right-to-erasure are API-only and erasure is implemented twice with different guarantees: apps/erp/src has no page or API call for /admin/backup or any erase route (features/admin contains only Users/Roles/Settings/Sessions/AuditLog/Notifications), so SPEC §2's owner-facing requirements are not reachable from the ERP. POST /customers/:id/erase (apps/api/src/modules/crm/customers.ts:338-370) anonymises only the customer, portal login and linked leads and hard-deletes co_applicants, while POST /admin/customers/:id/erase (apps/api/src/modules/admin/erase.ts:62-150) also handles site visits, enquiries, testimonials, document soft-delete and storage removal. Remove the weaker route, wire the admin one into the ERP, and add PII handling for audit before/after and login_events.identifier.
- **[B]** No infrastructure backup or restore drill, and the application role is the Postgres superuser: docker-compose.yml ships only a pgdata volume, infra/postgres/postgresql.conf has no archive_mode/WAL archiving, and docs/DEPLOY.md:54 defers to 'also schedule pg_dump'. The app export (apps/api/src/modules/admin/backup.ts:31,240) is assembled in memory and capped at 200,000 rows per table, so it is not a full backup for a large builder. Because the API connects as the superuser (docker-compose.yml:21) and apps/api/src/seed/run.ts:116-117 already uses `set local session_replication_role = replica` to bypass triggers, the audit/payments/demands no-delete triggers protect against bugs, not against the application role. Add WAL archiving + nightly base backups + an automated restore-and-verify job, and run the API as a non-superuser role.
- **[B]** Text search will not scale: every search is ILIKE '%term%' on full_name/email (apps/api/src/modules/crm/customers.ts:146; leads.ts:138; search/index.ts:30-46) or ILIKE 'term%' on phone/code, and there is no pg_trgm, text_pattern_ops or tsvector index anywhere (grep of packages, apps/api/src, docs). The (company_id, full_name) btree (packages/db/src/schema/customers.ts:64) cannot serve these, so at lakh-row tables they are per-company sequential scans; the 111 ms figure in docs/PERFORMANCE.md was measured on 12 customers. docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:103 promises monthly partitioning scripts in packages/db/partitions, which does not exist, and packages/db/src/schema/money.ts:166 carries a redundant index duplicating the ledger UNIQUE. Add trigram GIN indexes for contains-search, text_pattern_ops/citext for prefix search, drop the duplicate index, and load-test on a synthetic 5-lakh-customer dataset.
- **[B]** The database cannot refuse an inconsistent ledger or a duplicate customer; application code is the only guard: no CHECK that demands.cash+account = total or bookings.cash_agreed+account_agreed = agreement_value (only Zod refinements at packages/contracts/src/modules/bookings.ts:66,242); money also lives in untyped jsonb (packages/db/src/schema/json.ts:113-116,149,181-182); customer phone/PAN dedupe is an advisory lock + SELECT (apps/api/src/modules/crm/customerService.ts:203-227) over non-unique indexes (packages/db/src/schema/customers.ts:62-63) and the only concurrent-duplicate test covers leads, not customers (apps/api/test/integration/crm-adversarial.test.ts:136); ledger_entries is deliberately unguarded (packages/db/src/sql/0001_triggers.sql:24-26) and recompute is a destructive DELETE+INSERT (apps/api/src/modules/money/services/ledger.ts:240-242). Add the CHECK constraints, partial unique indexes on (company_id, phone) and (company_id, pan_hash) WHERE erased_at IS NULL, and a rebuild-epoch model for the ledger.
- **[C]** Website SSR is rate-limited as a single client: every public API GET is throttled per IP in one shared bucket (apps/api/src/plugins/auth.ts:113-116, PUBLIC_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MIN=300 in config.ts:44), and the Next.js server fetches the API over API_INTERNAL_URL with only an accept header and no forwarded visitor IP (apps/web/lib/api.ts:151-158; docker-compose.yml web). Detail pages are no-store and fetched twice per render (api.ts:183; docs/qa/web.md:67), so the public site's own fetches start receiving 429 at roughly 150 project-page views per minute. The same per-IP limiter will throttle real portal/website users behind Indian mobile CGNAT. Fix: forward X-Forwarded-For (or exempt the internal origin) and key public limits per route with a much higher ceiling.
- **[C]** No database timeouts and unbounded pool queueing: grep finds no statement_timeout, lock_timeout, idle_in_transaction_session_timeout or pool acquire timeout anywhere (packages/db/src/client.ts:92-100 sets only max/idle_timeout; infra/postgres/postgresql.conf sets none). Under overload requests queue on the pool until the 30 s client timeout (docs/PERFORMANCE.md p99 16.5 s), the opt-in API_MAX_INFLIGHT defaults to 0 (config.ts:50), and the pool math (2 replicas × API_WORKERS × 20 + worker 4 vs max_connections 200) already overflows at --scale api=3; the PgBouncer the docs promise (docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:104, DEPLOY.md:32) does not exist in any compose file.
- **[C]** Default list sorts and searches have no supporting index, so the measured 4 ms pages are a property of the 10-booking seed: GET /bookings sorts by -booking_date with only a company/scope filter (apps/api/src/modules/money/routes/bookings.ts:128-131) but the only date index is (company_id, project_id, status, booking_date) (packages/db/drizzle/0000_initial.sql:1579); GET /customers defaults to -created_at with no created_at index (crm/customers.ts:147; 0000_initial.sql:1589-1591); leads likewise (crm/leads.ts:96). All list searches are ILIKE '%term%' across names/phones/numbers (lib/list.ts:72-79) with no pg_trgm index (only jsonb GIN indexes exist, 0001_central_fixes.sql:6-9). At lakh-row tables each page becomes a full sort plus five joins, repeated for the count query (list.ts:187-189), and the > 10k estimated-count branch has never run on real data.
- **[C]** The shipped deployment is not the measured one and is not multi-instance safe: apps/api/Dockerfile:18-27 and docker-compose.yml set no API_WORKERS, so each container runs the single-process configuration (bookings p95 911 ms at 200 conns); RUN_JOBS_IN_API defaults true (config.ts:30) so api worker 0 also runs BullMQ jobs next to the dedicated worker container; STORAGE_DRIVER=s3 throws (apps/api/src/lib/storage.ts:34) so uploads and public media are tied to a local volume and streamed through the API (web/publicContent.ts:570-590); `deploy.replicas: 2` plus RUN_MIGRATIONS_ON_BOOT/SEED_ON_BOOT run drizzle migrations and the demo seed in both containers with no advisory lock and a check-then-act `count(*)` guard (server.ts:17-31,62-66; seed/run.ts:63-73); partitions (packages/db/partitions) and docker-compose.prod.yml claimed in ARCHITECTURE §6 do not exist; /metrics is per-process and locked behind a 15-minute admin JWT (lib/metrics.ts:1-8; modules/system/index.ts:49-58).
- **[C]** Redis is a hard dependency on every authenticated request and shares one evictable instance with security state and heavy work runs in the request path: loadStaff/loadPortal call app.redis.mget with no database fallback (plugins/auth.ts:70-106) on a client configured with maxRetriesPerRequest 3 (plugins/redis.ts:11), while docker-compose.yml runs a single Redis with --maxmemory 512mb --maxmemory-policy allkeys-lru --save "" that also stores revocation markers (auth.ts:46-48), idempotency locks and BullMQ queues — memory pressure can evict a session-revocation marker. Separately, sharp thumb/medium renditions (modules/documents/service.ts:128-139,188-196), pdfkit report/receipt rendering (modules/reports/routes/reports.ts:60-66, services/pdf.ts:11) and the 10,000-row CSV path that materialises every BookingRow and its related JSON before streaming (routes/bookings.ts:132-133; lib/list.ts:249-253) all run inside HTTP workers, contrary to ARCHITECTURE §6's 'heavy work never runs in a request'; no export job exists in JOB_NAMES (packages/contracts/src/enums.ts:785-794).
- **[D]** Privilege escalation (verified): any staff user whose role holds users.manage can PATCH /api/v1/users/{own id} with role_id = Super Admin and immediately holds all 45 permissions — apps/api/src/modules/users/index.ts:148-176 has no self-change or 'cannot grant more than you hold' guard; roles/index.ts lets custom roles carry any permission set.
- **[D]** Customer PII leak to low-privilege staff (verified): GET /api/v1/search is gated only by project.read, which every system role including the read-only Employee role holds, yet its CUSTOMER branch returns full name, customer code and full phone number (and the BOOKING branch booking numbers) with no customer.read/booking.read check and no project scope on customers — apps/api/src/modules/search/index.ts:40-47,68; packages/contracts/src/permissions.ts:217,301-304.
- **[D]** Default admin credentials and the full demo dataset are created on first production boot: config.ts:28 defaults SEED_ON_BOOT to true, docker-compose.yml:22/35 runs NODE_ENV=production with SEED_ON_BOOT:-true, and seed/bootstrap.ts:7-14,66-73 inserts admin@sgb.in / Admin@12345 plus five more with must_change_password=false; only `reseed` (seed/run.ts:131) is production-guarded.
- **[D]** Staff login leaks account existence and lockout is a remote denial-of-service lever (verified): a known e-mail with a wrong password returns details.attempts_left while an unknown e-mail returns no details (apps/api/src/modules/auth/staff.ts:78 vs :90), and five bad passwords from five different IPs locked the real user out (423 LOCKED) because lockout is counted per account only (auth/service.ts:188-200) — the 'indistinguishable on the wire' test (test/integration/security.test.ts:101-107) does not compare details.
- **[D]** Spreadsheet formula injection from the public website (verified): an unauthenticated POST /api/v1/public/enquiries with name `=HYPERLINK("http://evil","x")` is exported verbatim as a live formula by GET /api/v1/leads?format=csv — apps/api/src/lib/csv.ts:44-48 quotes but never neutralises leading = + - @ characters, and the same helper serves every CSV export (users, roles, audit, login-events). Related hardening items the owner should also know: the API connects to Postgres as a superuser (so the 'immutable' audit triggers can be disabled by the app role, as seed/run.ts:116 itself does), JWT/field-encryption key ids are hard-coded to 'k1' with no rotation path despite docs/SECURITY.md:30, GET /api/v1/openapi.json and /api/v1/docs are public in production (361 paths), and the ERP/portal web hosts ship with no Content-Security-Policy or frame-ancestors while keeping a 7-day refresh token in localStorage.
- **[E]** Agreement value is unguarded server-side: POST /bookings accepts any agreement_value_paise with only booking.create (apps/api/src/modules/money/services/bookings.ts:115-121; packages/contracts/src/modules/bookings.ts:65-75 checks only split = total and > 0), never compares it with the flat's price snapshot and records no deviation reason; the 'editable with permission' rule exists only in the ERP wizard (apps/erp/src/features/bookings/wizard/StepAgreement.tsx:72). Any Sales login can book a ₹1 Cr flat at ₹1 via the API.
- **[E]** Ledger order is not canonical: live appends are in posting order, but every rebuild (agreement revision bookings.ts:212, plan change :247, stage undo, integrity repair) re-sorts by business date where payments carry entry_date = payment_date and demands carry the IST date of posted_at (apps/api/src/modules/money/services/ledger.ts:128-136; packages/domain/src/ledger.ts:190-198, 355-368). A payment dated before an earlier-posted demand changes seq and intermediate running balances on the customer statement after any rebuild; rebuild() compares only aggregates/row count/final dues (ledger.ts:223-237) so integrity finding (e) never notices, and the domain agreement test pre-sorts events (packages/domain/test/ledger.test.ts:175).
- **[E]** Plan↔tower fit is checked only at assignment time: replacing a block's stage sequence (apps/api/src/modules/inventory/blocks.ts:245-252, 266-283), creating a block under a project-wide assignment, or editing a plan's installments (apps/api/src/modules/masters/kinds.ts:26-28 validates against master stages) can leave an ON_STAGE installment whose stage the tower lacks; it is then never raised (triggerDate null), the integrity report cannot flag it, and completeBooking (bookings.ts:289-294) compares raised − paid rather than agreed − paid, so a buyer can be handed over and the flat marked SOLD short of the agreed value.
- **[E]** Deadlock/serialisation edge on document numbering: nextSequence is an upsert whose (company, scope, fy) row lock is held to commit (packages/db/src/client.ts:138-151). A stage completion holds the demand-sequence row while locking further bookings (construction/service.ts:396 → money/services/dues.ts:123-135), whereas a concurrent dues run or booking creation locks the booking first and then asks for the same sequence row (dues.ts:158-188, bookings.ts:114-149) — opposite lock order, Postgres aborts one side and nothing in apps/api/src handles 40P01 or retries, so the loser is a 500. The same row also serialises every payment/booking of the company for the whole transaction.
- **[E]** Spec'd safety nets not wired: the 'at startup' dues catch-up and startup reconciliation (docs/SPEC.md:152,168) exist only as a registered job (apps/api/src/modules/admin/tools.ts:168) that server.ts/worker.ts never enqueue (worker.ts:22-26 registers cron only), so an outage across 00:30 IST leaves time-triggered dues unraised until the next night; expired HOLDs are never released (no sweep; createBooking bookings.ts:88 ignores hold_until); other-charge demand bells are keyed by 'booking' (money/services/dues.ts:118) so possession undo cannot withdraw them; and reversal on a COMPLETED booking leaves it COMPLETED with due > 0 and the flat SOLD (payments.ts:177-178, docs/TODO-integration.md #6-7).
- **[F]** Unbounded idempotency_keys growth: every ERP POST stores a row with a 24 h expires_at (apps/api/src/plugins/idempotency.ts:105-107) but no job or query ever deletes expired rows (apps/api/src/jobs/index.ts has no purge; only an index exists at packages/db/src/schema/identity.ts:188) — on a busy builder this table and its response_body jsonb grow forever.
- **[F]** 19 error codes raised by the API are missing from the contracts registry (e.g. TICKET_CLOSED at apps/api/src/modules/service/ticketService.ts:456, DUPLICATE_CUSTOMER at crm/customerService.ts:226, REOPEN_WINDOW_CLOSED, ALREADY_RATED), so packages/contracts/src/errors.ts ApiErrorSchema rejects them and the customer portal (apps/portal/src/api/client.ts:241-251) shows 'Something went wrong' with code UNKNOWN instead of the server's message; no test greps raised codes against ERROR_CODES.
- **[F]** CSV exports are silently truncated: format=csv runs synchronously capped at CSV_EXPORT_MAX_ROWS = 10 000 (apps/api/src/lib/list.ts:242-247) with no truncation header or warning and no worker export job (jobs/index.ts), contradicting docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:106; a finance user exporting a ledger/dues list above 10k rows gets an incomplete file. Dues, roznamcha, notifications and presets have no CSV at all.
- **[F]** The 'every mutable row has version; PATCH requires If-Match' claim (docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:52) is false: ten tables use the version-less timestamps() helper (packages/db/src/schema/_common.ts:24) and PATCH /customers/:id/co-applicants/:caId (apps/api/src/modules/crm/customers.ts:273) is an unconditional last-writer-wins update; 16 routes use optionalExpectedVersion (lib/version.ts:25-34) so stale state changes overwrite silently; PUT /booking-drafts (money/routes/bookings.ts:85-90) inserts a new row whenever the id is absent, making retries create duplicates.
- **[F]** No linter exists and formatting has drifted: every package's `lint` script is `tsc --noEmit`, there is no ESLint config or dependency anywhere, `prettier --check` reports 169 unformatted files in api/contracts/domain src, and .github/workflows/ci.yml never runs the 94 ERP unit tests, the portal build, a format check, coverage or any deploy stage — while README.md:33 claims CI runs every package's tests. Related: TRUST_PROXY defaults to true (apps/api/src/config.ts:44), so an API exposed without a proxy lets callers spoof X-Forwarded-For to choose their rate-limit and public-form idempotency scope.
- **[G]** Expired flat holds are never released: hold_until is validated on write (apps/api/src/modules/inventory/flats.ts:90-93, blocks.ts:400) but no job, trigger or effective-status read ever compares it to now (apps/api/src/jobs/index.ts:29-35 schedules no hold-expiry job). A lapsed hold keeps the flat HOLD indefinitely, hides it on the public availability grid (publicContent.ts:259-263), and blocks every other rep from booking or releasing it without settings.manage (flats.ts:103-105, money/services/bookings.ts:88-91).
- **[G]** ERP 'Enable portal for all' throws away the one-time credentials: the API returns temp passwords only in that response and never stores them (apps/api/src/modules/crm/portalAccess.ts:100-102,160), but apps/erp/src/features/crm/CustomersPage.tsx:44-48 shows only a count toast and the dialog (CustomersPage.tsx:159) tells staff to share passwords from each Customer 360 page, which cannot work. Bulk enable therefore creates logins nobody can use until each customer is individually reset; the flow was not browser-tested (docs/qa/erp.md 'Not covered').
- **[G]** Spec §13 startup sweep and startup reconciliation are not wired: NOTIFICATION_SWEEP is only an enum value (packages/contracts/src/enums.ts:790) with no registerJob/schedule, and STARTUP_RECONCILIATION is registered (apps/api/src/modules/admin/tools.ts:168) but never enqueued at boot (apps/api/src/server.ts, worker.ts:41). Bells pointing at soft-deleted documents or erased customers are never cleaned, and ledger/summary mismatches are only found by the 00:45 nightly job, not at restart.
- **[G]** Site-visit manage links are permanent bearer tokens: apps/api/src/modules/web/visitService.ts:186-189 looks visits up by an unexpiring sha256(token) hash, and apps/api/src/modules/web/publicVisits.ts:150 lets anyone holding the link cancel without login (rate-limited only). Add an expiry (e.g. visit end + 1 day) or rotate on reschedule; also add visitor self-reschedule, which the public routes (publicVisits.ts:127-150) do not offer.
- **[G]** KYC is a status field, not a workflow: apps/api/src/modules/crm/customers.ts:216-236 lets any customer.write holder set SUBMITTED with no PAN/Aadhaar document attached, and VERIFIED→SUBMITTED or REJECTED→VERIFIED moves are unrestricted (only VERIFIED/REJECTED require customer.kyc_view). Combined with the booking wizard never reserving the flat and having only a beforeunload guard (apps/erp/src/features/bookings/wizard/BookingWizard.tsx:139-149; FlatPickerGrid.tsx:16-18), the sales desk can lose a flat mid-entry and book against an unevidenced KYC.
- **[H]** Production first boot seeds the demo builder: docker-compose.yml:35 `SEED_ON_BOOT: ${SEED_ON_BOOT:-true}` under NODE_ENV=production, apps/api/src/config.ts:28 defaults to true, and apps/api/src/server.ts:64-66/103 seeds whenever the company has no projects — twelve demo customers (Welcome@123) and six staff logins (Admin@12345) land in a real database unless the operator remembers to override; the reseed guards (tools.ts:267-271, run.ts:131) do not cover this path.
- **[H]** No durable storage, no restore path: apps/api/src/lib/storage.ts:34 throws for STORAGE_DRIVER=s3 although docs/DEPLOY.md:34 and .env.example:20-26 instruct operators to use it; uploads (KYC, receipts, photos) live on a single Docker volume shared by two API replicas and the worker, so the stack cannot run on more than one host; docs/DEPLOY.md:54 references a restore.md that does not exist and compose has no pg_dump job. Related: packages/db/partitions and docker-compose.prod.yml promised in docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:103-104 are absent.
- **[H]** Failed jobs are silent: apps/api/src/jobs/index.ts:63-68 catches every handler error and resolves with status FAILED, so BullMQ never retries and worker.ts:43's failed handler never fires; /metrics needs a staff JWT with admin.tools (apps/api/src/modules/system/index.ts:51) and is per-process under node:cluster; the ERP has no page for GET /admin/jobs or /admin/system (grep in apps/erp/src → none), so a failed nightly DAILY_DUES or integrity run is invisible to staff; business modules log 4 lines in total and there is no tracing, error reporter or alert hook.
- **[H]** WCAG AA is baked out at the token level and never measured: portal inkSubtle #8B95A5 on white 3.03:1 (apps/portal/src/ui/theme.ts:25, 12 px captions), portal gold chip gold700-on-gold100 2.94:1 (theme.ts:96), web gold-600 eyebrows on ivory 3.19:1 and ink-soft 3.96:1 (apps/web/app/globals.css:24,42), ERP white-on-#ef4444 danger buttons 3.76:1 (apps/erp/src/components/ui/button.tsx:16, index.css:63); portal inputs have no accessibilityLabel and errors no live region (apps/portal/src/ui/Field.tsx:18-47); docs/qa/erp.md:67 records contrast as 'visual check only' and no axe/pa11y runs in CI. ERP phone targets are 44 px, the spec asks for 48.
- **[H]** Customer documents and the staff app have polish/hardening gaps a builder will notice on day one: apps/api/assets/fonts does not exist, so apps/api/src/modules/reports/services/pdf.ts:66-91 prints 'Rs. 12,50,000' instead of ₹ on every receipt, demand letter, allotment letter and statement; apps/erp/src/lib/format.ts:218-221 formatDate renders instants in the browser's zone (CustomerDetailPage.tsx:345, RateCardsTab.tsx:145-146, CustomerStatementPage.tsx:202); and the ERP/portal origins are served with no Content-Security-Policy or X-Frame-Options (apps/erp/nginx.conf, apps/portal/nginx.conf, infra/caddy/Caddyfile:8-22; API helmet CSP off at apps/api/src/app.ts:63) while the ERP keeps its refresh token in localStorage (apps/erp/src/lib/api.ts:5,12).

---

## Group A — Architecture & Backend — **7405 / 10,000** (previous implementation: 4510)

| # | Factor | Previous (v1) | First score | Final score |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Service architecture & modularity | 505 | 810 | **770** |
| 2 | Framework choice & async correctness | 500 | 720 | **730** |
| 3 | Transaction integrity / atomicity | 385 | 850 | **835** |
| 4 | Domain logic isolation | 520 | 840 | **830** |
| 5 | Error handling & error envelope consistency | 660 | 700 | **700** |
| 6 | Configuration & environment management | 400 | 760 | **700** |
| 7 | Background jobs & scheduling robustness | 300 | 680 | **520** |
| 8 | Dependency hygiene | 240 | 760 | **750** |
| 9 | Code quality: readability, duplication, function size, naming, dead code | 460 | 760 | **760** |
| 10 | Type safety & validation | 540 | 820 | **810** |

### A1. Service architecture & modularity — 770/1000 (v1: 505)

**Strengths.** Clean monorepo layering with a hard dependency direction, a module-plugin convention with a loader, a route() helper that makes permission coverage mechanically checkable, and genuinely zero import cycles. Business modules expose named service functions that other modules call rather than touching tables ad hoc.

**Gaps.** Cross-module coupling goes through internal file paths (construction and reports import money/common.js and money/services/*.js directly) instead of the index 'service surface', so the boundary is documentary, not enforced. portal is a hub depending on five modules, and reports depends on money in nine places. The ARCHITECTURE.md module map and the per-module routes/service/queries convention do not match the actual folders, and several route files mix HTTP handling with orchestration (registerBlockRoutes is 368 lines). No tooling (dependency-cruiser/eslint boundaries) guards the boundaries.

**Second auditor.** The first auditor's positives hold: route() really does enforce exactly one of permission|audience|public (apps/api/src/lib/routes.ts:88-89), the registry feeds a CI test (test/integration/rbac.test.ts:41), the dependency direction contracts<-domain<-db<-api is real (package.json files) and my own scan of 140 files found 0 runtime import cycles. But the boundary is weaker than 810 implies: construction and admin reach into money/services/*.js and money/common.js past the documented index surface, and - the finding the first auditor missed - job-handler registration is coupled to Fastify plugin registration (registerMoneyJobs(app) is called from moneyModule(app)), so the architecture's 'separate worker process' (ARCHITECTURE.md:14,106) cannot see any business handler. The ARCHITECTURE.md module map (pdf/cms/public/visits with routes.ts/service.ts/queries.ts) does not describe the real tree (reports/web/inventory/money, no queries.ts anywhere). No tooling guards the boundaries.

**World-best would.** Enforce module boundaries with a linter rule or package-per-module exports (only index.ts importable), keep route files thin over services, and keep the architecture doc generated from or tested against the real module list.

Evidence: `apps/api/src/app.ts:107-120 (core modules wired explicitly, business modules auto-discovered under /api/v1 by modules/loader.ts:257-313 with KKT_MODULES filter)`, `apps/api/src/lib/routes.ts:183-209 (single route() helper enforces exactly one of permission|audience|public and feeds the route registry at :212-224)`, `packages/*/package.json: domain has zero dependencies, contracts depends only on zod, db depends on contracts; dependency direction contracts <- domain <- db <- api holds`, `Import-cycle scan of all 140 files under apps/api/src: 0 runtime cycles; module-level graph is acyclic (reports->money x9, money->notifications x5, portal->{service,documents,money,reports,construction})`, `apps/api/src/modules/construction/service.ts:19-21 imports ../money/common.js and ../money/services/dues.js directly, bypassing the documented 'service surface' in modules/money/index.ts:5-28`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md §4 lists modules bookings/payments/dues/customers/leads/cms/public/visits with routes.ts/service.ts/queries.ts, but the tree is money/crm/web/inventory with no queries.ts anywhere and route files that also hold orchestration (inventory/blocks.ts, 483 lines)`

### A2. Framework choice & async correctness — 730/1000 (v1: 500)

**Strengths.** Fastify 5 with compiled Zod validation/serialisation is the right engine; argon2, sharp and CSV export are non-blocking, search queries are cancelled on timeout, and load shedding exists at two levels. Measured 200-connection p95 is under 300 ms for every list/detail except the bookings page.

**Gaps.** pdfkit document construction is synchronous JavaScript executed in request handlers (receipts, demand letters, statements, 2,000-row report PDFs), so a burst of PDF downloads stalls that worker's event loop. The admin backup compresses with deflateRawSync in-request, and the admin 'run now' endpoint executes nightly jobs inline in the HTTP request. ARCHITECTURE.md states heavy work (PDF, thumbnails) never runs in a request and names thumbnail/PDF-prewarm jobs that do not exist. At 1,000 zero-think-time connections list endpoints miss the 300 ms p95 (0.5–1.7 s) on the shared 4-vCPU box.

**Second auditor.** Verified: Fastify 5 + Zod type provider, under-pressure, compress, helmet (app.ts:50-83); the in-flight cap plugin exists (plugins/loadShed.ts:11-27, note the file is 37 lines, not the lines cited); sharp and argon2 are async; CSV streams. The first auditor was too harsh on the backup: table dumps go through zlib createDeflateRaw streams with back-pressure (admin/zip.ts:169-199, backup.ts:191), and only manifest.json/RESTORE.md use deflateRawSync (zip.ts:86, 268-269). Still in-request on the event loop: JSON.stringify + JS crc32 over up to 200,000 rows per table, pdfkit document construction for receipts/demand letters/allotment letters/statements (documents.ts finish() x4) and 2,000-row report PDFs (finishStream), and the admin 'run now' route executes DAILY_DUES/NIGHTLY_INTEGRITY inline for up to 600 s (admin/tools.ts:236-250). None of the four PDF routes carries a rateLimit (reports/routes/documents.ts:17-44). Measured: 200-connection p95 < 300 ms for all lists except bookings (404 ms); 1,000 connections 0.5-1.7 s on lists (PERFORMANCE.md:36-46,52-53).

**World-best would.** Move PDF/ZIP generation to the BullMQ worker (or a worker_threads pool) with signed-link delivery, enqueue admin job runs instead of executing them in-request, and keep every request handler free of synchronous CPU work beyond a few milliseconds.

Evidence: `apps/api/src/app.ts:50-101 (Fastify 5 with Zod type provider, under-pressure, compress, helmet) and plugins/loadShed.ts:125-141 (in-flight cap with 503 + Retry-After)`, `apps/api/src/modules/reports/services/documents.ts:44,80,154,237 build receipt/demand/allotment/statement PDFs with pdfkit inside request handlers; services/pdf.ts:472-480 finish() buffers the whole document; PDF_MAX_ROWS=2000 at pdf.ts:523`, `apps/api/src/modules/admin/zip.ts:86 deflateRawSync and backup.ts:394-410: the POST /admin/backup handler builds a ZIP of up to 200,000 rows per table synchronously in the request`, `apps/api/src/modules/admin/tools.ts:236-250 runs DAILY_DUES/NIGHTLY_INTEGRITY via runJob() inline in the HTTP request instead of enqueueing`, `apps/api/src/modules/documents/service.ts:124-139 generates sharp thumb/medium variants during the upload request (async libvips, so non-blocking, but still in-request); no thumbnail or PDF-prewarm job exists despite docs/ARCHITECTURE.md §1/§6 claims`, `apps/api/src/lib/csv.ts:60-72 streams CSV via async generator; lib/password.ts:7-13 argon2 async; search cancels server-side on timeout per docs/PERFORMANCE.md`

### A3. Transaction integrity / atomicity — 835/1000 (v1: 385)

**Strengths.** Real PostgreSQL transactions with explicit row + advisory locking, gapless numbering allocated inside the unit of work, DB-level uniqueness and append-only triggers rather than application checks, and compensation for the one non-transactional resource (object storage). The worked ledger example and concurrent-booking scenarios are integration-tested and pass locally.

**Gaps.** Idempotency is not crash-safe: the stored response is written after commit in onSend and is best-effort (errors only logged), so a crash or Redis/DB failure between commit and store lets a client retry with the same key and post a duplicate payment; payments.idempotency_key is not unique to backstop this. All transactions run at READ COMMITTED with no serializable/retry path, so correctness depends on every writer remembering to take the booking lock. Redis-side claims (overdue notification NX keys) are not atomic with the DB write.

**Second auditor.** Verified in code: postPayment is one db.transaction covering FOR UPDATE booking load, lockBooking (row lock then pg_advisory_xact_lock, money/common.ts:77-80), bucket cap, gapless receipt via INSERT..ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE RETURNING (packages/db/src/client.ts:139-151), payment insert, ledger append, audit and notification (payments.ts:71-127); append-only/no-delete triggers and partial unique indexes exist (0001_triggers.sql:7-45,80-88); storeBuffer compensates object storage on DB failure (documents/service.ts:124-168); idempotency and money tests pass on a fresh database. The gaps are real and I confirmed them: the idempotent response is stored in onSend after commit and errors are only logged (plugins/idempotency.ts:100-118), payments.idempotency_key is a plain text column with no unique index (schema/money.ts:116, index list at :126-130 has none), there is no 40001/40P01 handling anywhere, and the overdue-notification job claims the Redis NX key before the DB transaction (money/jobs.ts:63-64) so a failed transaction loses that day's notice. The idempotency tests (4) do not exercise the commit-then-store-failure window. I take 15 points more than the first auditor for the unguarded duplicate-payment window on a money path.

**World-best would.** Persist the idempotency claim (scope, key, hash) inside the business transaction and enforce it with a unique index on the payment row, add a serialization-failure retry wrapper for money paths, and move cross-store side effects to a transactional outbox.

Evidence: `apps/api/src/modules/money/services/payments.ts:71-127 postPayment: one db.transaction covering lock, validation, gapless receipt number, payment insert, ledger append, summary, audit and notification`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/common.ts:72-80 lockBooking: booking row FOR UPDATE then pg_advisory_xact_lock with documented lock order; ledger.ts:247-257 appendEvent runs under it; packages/db/src/client.ts:133-151 advisory lock + upsert sequence`, `packages/db/src/sql/0001_triggers.sql:7-45 append-only audit_logs and no-delete payments/demands triggers; :80-88 partial unique indexes (one active booking per flat, non-void demand seq)`, `apps/api/src/modules/documents/service.ts:124-168 storeBuffer compensates storage writes (removes written keys) when the DB insert fails`, `apps/api/src/plugins/idempotency.ts:100-118 stores the idempotent response in onSend after the handler's transaction has committed, and packages/db/src/schema/money.ts:116 payments.idempotency_key has no unique index`, `Spot-check: test/integration/money.test.ts (32 tests incl. concurrency on one booking) and idempotency.test.ts (4) pass on a fresh database kkt_audit_A`

### A4. Domain logic isolation — 830/1000 (v1: 520)

**Strengths.** Every algorithm the spec names (flat generation, pricing, plan validation, telescoping dues, two-bucket ledger, bucket cap, slots, FY numbering, money words) is a pure function in packages/domain with exhaustive tests, and the API's money services are thin I/O wrappers around them. Domain types are also reused by the API for ledger events and schedules.

**Gaps.** A few money calculations leaked into the API layer (demandAmountsFor telescoping, summary reconstruction, event ordering Clock) and are tested only indirectly. Some route files (inventory/blocks.ts, web/publicContent.ts, portal/bookings.ts) carry substantial orchestration in handlers rather than services, and validation rules such as payment-date windows and plan applicability live in services rather than the engine, so they are not unit-testable without a database.

**Second auditor.** Confirmed: packages/domain has zero runtime dependencies, 124 tests pass locally, index.ts exports money/dates/ist/fy/canonical/flatGeneration/pricing/paymentPlan/dues/ledger/visits/sla, and the only impurity is todayIST's default clock (ist.ts:53). API money services wrap domain functions (appendEvent -> appendLedgerRow, assertBucketCap -> bucketCapCheck). The first auditor's 'demandAmountsFor re-implements telescoping' is partly overstated: it composes domain primitives (toBasisPoints/bpOfPaise) rather than hand-rolling rounding (ledger.ts:113-120), but the derivation rule itself still lives in the API and is only indirectly tested. Route files holding 368-line registration functions (inventory/blocks.ts registerBlockRoutes, measured) and payment-date/plan-applicability validation living in services rather than the engine are confirmed. A notch below the first auditor because the money rebuild path (sourceEvents) is a sizeable algorithm in the API layer.

**World-best would.** Keep every rupee computation in packages/domain (including demand amount derivation) and keep route handlers to parse-call-serialise, with services holding only transaction and lock orchestration.

Evidence: `packages/domain/src/index.ts (money, dates, ist, fy, canonical, flatGeneration, pricing, paymentPlan, dues, ledger, visits, sla); package.json has zero dependencies; only impure default is todayIST(now = new Date()) at ist.ts:53`, `packages/domain/src/ledger.ts:340 appendLedgerRow and deriveLedger, dues.ts:245 evaluateDues, 334 schedulePreview; 124 unit tests pass (pnpm --filter @kkt/domain test)`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/services/ledger.ts:247-257 and payments.ts:36-42 orchestrate I/O around domain functions (appendLedgerRow, bucketCapCheck)`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/services/ledger.ts:268-276 demandAmountsFor re-implements telescoping demand rounding in the API layer ('same rounding as the dues engine') instead of calling a domain function`, `apps/api/src/modules/inventory/blocks.ts: registerBlockRoutes is 368 lines with orchestration inside handlers; web/publicContent.ts registerPublicContentRoutes 158 lines`, `packages/domain/src/index.ts:1-14 and package.json dependencies {} - verified zero deps`

### A5. Error handling & error envelope consistency — 700/1000 (v1: 660)

**Strengths.** One error envelope everywhere, including Fastify-internal errors, body-too-large, validation issues with paths, response-serialisation failures (500 rather than leaking a mismatched body) and load-shed 503s with Retry-After; business errors carry stable codes and details, and 401 ApiError call sites show consistent use.

**Gaps.** The contracts error registry has drifted from what the API emits: 18 codes are missing or spelled differently (INSUFFICIENT_CASH vs CASH_INSUFFICIENT, UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA vs UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA_TYPE), and because the portal client validates envelopes with the enum, customers hitting closed-ticket/reopen/rating conflicts get a generic 'UNKNOWN' message instead of the server's explanation. Per-code details schemas (BucketCapDetails) do not match the payload actually sent. ApiError.code is an untyped string and no test asserts emitted codes are a subset of ERROR_CODES, which is how the drift went unnoticed.

**Second auditor.** The central handler is as described: ApiError, Zod validation issues, response-serialization failure -> 500 (never leaks a mismatched body), 503 with Retry-After, body-too-large/invalid-media mapping, 404 envelope (lib/errors.ts:59-96). My own diff of emitted codes vs packages/contracts ERROR_CODES finds 19 unregistered codes (one more than the first auditor: PAYMENT_NOT_CLEARED and PAYMENT_NOT_PENDING in addition to the 17 shared ones; INSUFFICIENT_CASH vs registry CASH_INSUFFICIENT and UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA vs UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA_TYPE are spelling drift). Because ApiErrorSchema uses the strict ErrorCodeSchema (errors.ts:150-157) and the portal validates with isApiError (portal client.ts:241-251), customers hitting TICKET_CLOSED/REOPEN_WINDOW_CLOSED/ALREADY_RATED get a generic 'Something went wrong'. Mitigation the first auditor did not credit: the ERP client's isEnvelope is structural, not enum-based (apps/erp/src/lib/api.ts:84-130), so staff see the real message. BucketCapDetailsSchema mismatch confirmed (received_paise/attempted_paise vs paid_paise/amount_paise at payments.ts:41). No test asserts emitted codes are a subset of the registry (grep ERROR_CODES in apps/api/test: none). 700 stands.

**World-best would.** Type ApiError.code as ErrorCode, derive the registry from the same source the API throws from, assert in CI that every emitted code and details shape validates against contracts, and have clients validate loosely with a typed fallback rather than discarding the message.

Evidence: `apps/api/src/lib/errors.ts:59-96 central errorHandler maps ApiError, Zod validation (details.issues), serialization failures, 503 with Retry-After, 5xx and unknown routes to one envelope {error:{code,message,details,request_id}}`, `apps/api/src/lib/errors.ts:7 ApiError.code is typed string, not ErrorCode; lib/pgErrors.ts:337-342 translates 23505/23503`, `packages/contracts/src/errors.ts:4-49 ERROR_CODES; grep of emitted codes shows 18 not in the registry: TICKET_CLOSED, REOPEN_WINDOW_CLOSED, ALREADY_RATED, UNDO_NOT_LATEST, REGENERATE_BLOCKED, PARKING_NOT_AVAILABLE, JOB_ALREADY_RUNNING, HAS_ACTIVE_BOOKING, DUPLICATE_LEAD/CUSTOMER/PRESET, ALREADY_DEMANDED, SUPER_ADMIN_ONLY, PUBLISH_REQUIREMENTS, NOT_IMPLEMENTED, INSUFFICIENT_CASH (registry: CASH_INSUFFICIENT), UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA (registry: UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA_TYPE)`, `apps/portal/src/api/client.ts:241-251 uses isApiError (strict enum) so portal-reachable TICKET_CLOSED / REOPEN_WINDOW_CLOSED / ALREADY_RATED (service/ticketService.ts:456,637,656) collapse to code UNKNOWN with a generic message`, `packages/contracts/src/errors.ts:126-132 BucketCapDetailsSchema expects received_paise/attempted_paise but money/services/payments.ts:41 sends paid_paise/amount_paise`, `Own comm(1) of emitted vs registry: ALREADY_DEMANDED ALREADY_RATED DUPLICATE_CUSTOMER DUPLICATE_LEAD DUPLICATE_PRESET HAS_ACTIVE_BOOKING INSUFFICIENT_CASH JOB_ALREADY_RUNNING NOT_IMPLEMENTED PARKING_NOT_AVAILABLE PAYMENT_NOT_CLEARED PAYMENT_NOT_PENDING PUBLISH_REQUIREMENTS REGENERATE_BLOCKED REOPEN_WINDOW_CLOSED SUPER_ADMIN_ONLY TICKET_CLOSED UNDO_NOT_LATEST UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA (19)`

### A6. Configuration & environment management — 700/1000 (v1: 400)

**Strengths.** Configuration is a single validated Zod schema with sane ranges, insecure defaults are impossible in production, every variable is documented, the image runs as a non-root user and readiness checks real dependencies. Runtime knobs (workers, pool, prepare, in-flight cap, rate limits) are explicit and explained.

**Gaps.** Startup validation is incomplete: STORAGE_DRIVER=s3 is accepted but the adapter does not exist and the failure surfaces on the first upload, not at boot. The documented JWT key rotation is not implemented (one derived key, fixed kid). TRUST_PROXY=true by default means X-Forwarded-For is trusted unless an operator remembers to turn it off. Several environment variables bypass the schema, the production compose seeds demo data by default and points API_PUBLIC_URL at the ERP domain, and secrets are plain environment strings with no secrets-manager or rotation story.

**Second auditor.** The Zod EnvSchema with min secret lengths and production-only refusal of DEV_DEFAULTS is verified (config.ts:4-75), as are the non-root multi-stage image and documented variables. But I found more unsafe defaults than the first auditor: TRUST_PROXY defaults to true and lib/rateLimit.ts clientIp() takes the LEFTMOST X-Forwarded-For hop, which the client controls even behind a real proxy, so per-IP rate limits, login lockout keys and the idempotency scope for public routes (req.subjectId ?? req.clientIp) are spoofable by header; DATABASE_URL and REDIS_URL carry localhost/postgres:postgres defaults that also apply in production; docker-compose runs api with deploy.replicas: 2 while RUN_MIGRATIONS_ON_BOOT and SEED_ON_BOOT are true and neither migrateHandle nor seedDemo takes an advisory lock, so two replicas race on CREATE EXTENSION/migrations and the check-then-insert demo seed; STORAGE_DRIVER=s3 is accepted by the schema but createStorage throws only on first upload while DEPLOY.md:34 tells operators to set it; JWT rotation promised in .env.example:9 is a single derived key with fixed kid k1 (lib/jwt.ts:18-31). KKT_MODULES, KKT_WORKER_INDEX, DATABASE_PREPARE and KKT_*_DIR bypass the schema.

**World-best would.** Validate every dependency (storage driver, replica URL, S3 credentials) at boot with a fail-fast probe, implement key rotation with a kid-indexed key set, route all env reads through the schema, and default to the safer value (TRUST_PROXY=false, SEED_ON_BOOT=false) in production.

Evidence: `apps/api/src/config.ts:4-77 Zod EnvSchema with ranges and min secret lengths; :57-61 DEV_DEFAULTS applied only when NODE_ENV!=production; :64-75 loadConfig throws 'Invalid configuration' at import`, `apps/api/src/lib/storage.ts:33-36 STORAGE_DRIVER=s3 throws only when first used (documents/service.ts:41-45 lazy storage()), so a misconfigured production box passes startup and fails on the first upload; docs/DEPLOY.md:34 presents S3 as configurable`, `apps/api/src/lib/jwt.ts:18-27 derives a single Ed25519 key with fixed kid 'k1' from JWT_SECRET; .env.example:9 claims 'Rotate by adding a new key id' but no previous-key verification exists`, `apps/api/src/config.ts:44 TRUST_PROXY defaults to true; packages/db/src/client.ts:34-36 DATABASE_PREPARE and modules/loader.ts:58 KKT_MODULES, server.ts:99 KKT_WORKER_INDEX, migrate.ts:22-35 KKT_* dirs are read ad hoc outside the schema`, `docker-compose.yml:20-39 api service: secrets via environment, SEED_ON_BOOT defaults true in the production compose, API_PUBLIC_URL set to the ERP host; apps/api/Dockerfile:17-26 multi-stage, non-root user`, `apps/api/src/modules/system/index.ts:13-44 /health and /ready probe database and Redis; .env.example documents every variable inline`

### A7. Background jobs & scheduling robustness — 520/1000 (v1: 300)

**Strengths.** Durable Redis-backed BullMQ queue with idempotent job schedulers (safe across N API instances and a dedicated worker), IST cron, a job_runs audit table, Redis-NX dedupe for once-per-day notifications, and idempotent handlers that tolerate overlapping runs thanks to per-booking locks.

**Gaps.** There is no retry path at all: runJob swallows handler exceptions into job_runs, so a transient DB failure during DAILY_DUES leaves installments unraised until the next night with nothing but a log line and no alert or dead-letter. The admin 'run now' route bypasses the queue and runs long jobs inside an HTTP request (enqueueJob is dead code). The job-name enum contains an orphan (NOTIFICATION_SWEEP) that would 'succeed' as a no-op, two job names are cast past the enum, and the thumbnail/PDF-prewarm jobs the architecture promises do not exist. No per-job timeout, stalled-job tuning or failure alerting is configured.

**Second auditor.** The first auditor's gaps are all confirmed (runJob swallows handler errors into job_runs so BullMQ never retries - no attempts/backoff anywhere; admin run-now executes inline and enqueueJob is dead; NOTIFICATION_SWEEP has no handler; two names are cast past the enum; thumbnail/PDF-prewarm jobs do not exist). But the decisive defect was missed: the standalone worker entry (apps/api/src/worker.ts, the docker-compose 'worker' service running node dist/worker.js) imports only jobs/index.js and never loads a single module, so its handler registry is empty and every job it consumes runs the placeholder that logs 'not implemented yet' and records job_runs OK {implemented:false}. In the shipped compose topology that worker competes with the API's in-process workers for the same queue, so a share of nightly DAILY_DUES / OVERDUE_NOTIFICATIONS runs silently do nothing while reporting success; with RUN_JOBS_IN_API=false (the documented alternative in .env.example) 100% of scheduled jobs are no-ops. Additionally PORTAL_FILE_CLEANUP is registered but absent from JOB_SCHEDULES, and STARTUP_RECONCILIATION is registered but never run at startup or on a schedule. The durable queue, IST cron, idempotent schedulers and per-booking locks remain genuine strengths.

**World-best would.** Let handler errors propagate so BullMQ retries with exponential backoff and a dead-letter queue, enqueue admin-triggered runs, alert on FAILED job_runs, and keep the job registry and enum generated from one list with a test that every name has a handler.

Evidence: `apps/api/src/worker.ts:331-336 upsertJobScheduler per JOB_SCHEDULES (jobs/index.ts:27-34, cron in Asia/Kolkata) and :351 BullMQ Worker concurrency 2; separate worker process in docker-compose.yml:40-52 plus RUN_JOBS_IN_API in worker 0 (server.ts:99-101)`, `apps/api/src/jobs/index.ts:54-69 runJob catches every error and returns {status:'FAILED'} so BullMQ sees success; no attempts/backoff anywhere in apps/api/src (grep), so failed jobs are never retried and worker.on('failed') never fires for handler errors`, `apps/api/src/modules/admin/tools.ts:236-250 POST /admin/jobs/:name/run executes runJob inline in the HTTP request under a 600 s Redis lock; worker.ts:339-342 enqueueJob is never called`, `packages/contracts/src/enums.ts:786-795 JOB_NAMES includes NOTIFICATION_SWEEP with no handler or schedule; jobs/index.ts:44-46 unknown names run a placeholder returning OK {implemented:false}; service/jobs.ts:15 and portal/files.ts:26 cast names 'as JobName'`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/jobs.ts:41-53 overdue dedupe via Redis SET NX with DB fallback; service/jobs.ts:106-112 idempotent UPDATE guards; per-booking locks (money/common.ts:72-80) make overlapping dues runs safe`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md §1 lists thumbnail generation and PDF prewarm as BullMQ jobs; no such handlers are registered (registerJob sites: DAILY_DUES, NIGHTLY_INTEGRITY, OVERDUE_NOTIFICATIONS, STARTUP_RECONCILIATION, VISIT_OUTCOME_NUDGE, SLA_BREACH_CHECK, PORTAL_FILE_CLEANUP)`

### A8. Dependency hygiene — 750/1000 (v1: 240)

**Strengths.** The dependency set is lean and relevant for an ERP: no AI SDKs, payment gateways, data-science or duplicate web frameworks; business logic has no third-party deps at all; the lockfile is frozen in CI and native builds are explicitly allow-listed; the API ships as a bundled image with only native modules external.

**Gaps.** Four API dependencies (plus one types package) are declared but never imported, ranges are not pinned, and there is no vulnerability audit, update bot, or SBOM in CI. shamefully-hoist removes the strict-resolution benefit that is the main supply-chain argument for pnpm, and the portal pins different TypeScript and React majors/minors from the rest of the workspace, inviting duplicate React copies in a hoisted tree.

**Second auditor.** Verified by grep: @fastify/static, @fastify/rate-limit, ics and luxon (plus @types/luxon) have zero import sites in apps/api/src yet are declared; .npmrc sets node-linker=hoisted + shamefully-hoist=true, discarding pnpm's strict resolution; all ranges are caret/tilde with the lockfile frozen in CI (ci.yml:30); CI has no pnpm audit, Renovate/Dependabot, license or SBOM step; root package.json lists sharp twice in onlyBuiltDependencies; portal pins react 19.3.0 and typescript ~6.0.3 against ^19.1.0 / ~5.9.3 elsewhere. The positives are also real: the set is lean and ERP-relevant, domain has zero deps, contracts only zod, native builds allow-listed, API bundled with only native modules external. Slightly lower than the first auditor because 'lint' being tsc means nothing would ever flag the unused packages.

**World-best would.** Remove the unused packages, run pnpm audit (or socket/osv) and a license check in CI with Renovate for updates, use pnpm's strict linker with explicit peer deps, align versions across apps, and generate an SBOM per image.

Evidence: `apps/api/package.json dependencies: 29 purpose-fit packages (fastify plugins, drizzle, postgres, bullmq, ioredis, jose, argon2, otplib, pdfkit, sharp, zod); packages/domain/package.json has zero runtime dependencies`, `Unused in apps/api/src (zero import sites): @fastify/static, @fastify/rate-limit, ics, luxon (+ @types/luxon); root package.json:30 lists 'sharp' twice in onlyBuiltDependencies`, `.npmrc: node-linker=hoisted + shamefully-hoist=true (disables pnpm's phantom-dependency isolation); all ranges are caret/tilde, pinned only by pnpm-lock.yaml with --frozen-lockfile in .github/workflows/ci.yml:30`, `No pnpm audit, Dependabot/Renovate or license/SBOM step: .github contains only workflows/ci.yml`, `apps/portal/package.json: typescript ~6.0.3 and react 19.3.0 vs ~5.9.3 and ^19.1.0 in erp/web (version skew under a hoisted node_modules); apps/api/tsup.config.ts bundles workspace packages and externalises native sharp/argon2/pdfkit`, `apps/api/package.json:25,27,36,39 declare @fastify/rate-limit, @fastify/static, ics, luxon; grep 'from "<pkg>' in apps/api/src: 0 hits each`

### A9. Code quality: readability, duplication, function size, naming, dead code — 760/1000 (v1: 460)

**Strengths.** The code is consistently named, densely documented with intent-revealing JSDoc, free of TODO/any/console noise, and built on a small set of well-designed helpers (route registry, pagination, optimistic versioning, masters CRUD factory) that remove a lot of boilerplate; ownership comments and spec section references make the money paths easy to follow.

**Gaps.** There is no linter or formatter gate — 'lint' is just tsc — and the house style of 200–400-character one-liners (a payment audit+notify pair on a single line) hurts review and diffability. Several route-registration functions run to hundreds of lines, masking helpers are re-implemented twice despite living in the domain package, and small copy-paste patterns recur (If-Match parsing, 31 identical insert guards). Dead exports (enqueueJob, createDbCluster, publicJwks, a placeholder job handler) and stale comments contradicting the code show the finishing pass did not sweep for leftovers.

**Second auditor.** Measurements agree with the first auditor in substance: 937 lines over 200 chars and 151 over 300 in 24,415 lines of API source, registerBlockRoutes 368 lines, largest files 671/622/603 lines, 28 identical 'returned no row' guards, no ESLint config anywhere ('lint' = tsc --noEmit in every package), 0 'as any', 0 console.* outside CLIs, zero import cycles. Two of their claims do not survive: PAN/Aadhaar masking is NOT re-implemented - crm/customerService.ts:6,78,83 and portal/profile.ts:12,28 import maskPAN/maskAadhaar from @kkt/domain and only wrap decryption; and createDbCluster is exercised by packages/db tests (unused in app code only). enqueueJob and publicJwks are confirmed dead, the SLA_BREACH_JOB comment is stale (enums.ts:792 has it), and the worker/registry split (factor 7) is itself a readability trap: a reader of worker.ts cannot see that handlers come from Fastify plugins. Net: 760 is right.

**World-best would.** Add ESLint (typescript-eslint, import/boundaries, max-lines-per-function) and Prettier checks to CI, split route files into thin handlers plus services, delete dead exports, and make duplicated helpers single-sourced from the domain package.

Evidence: `apps/api/package.json:11 and every package: lint = 'tsc --noEmit'; no ESLint config anywhere in the repo; .prettierrc printWidth 120 is not checked in CI — 959 lines in apps/api/src exceed 200 chars and 155 exceed 300 (e.g. money/services/payments.ts:123-124)`, `Function size: inventory/blocks.ts registerBlockRoutes 368 lines, web/publicContent.ts registerPublicContentRoutes 158 lines; largest files service/ticketService.ts 671, web/publicContent.ts 622, construction/service.ts 603`, `Duplication: PAN/Aadhaar masking re-implemented in crm/customerService.ts:76-87 and portal/profile.ts:24-41 although packages/domain/src/canonical.ts:43,127,133 export maskPAN/maskAadhaar/maskAccount; lib/version.ts:13-34 expectedVersion/optionalExpectedVersion copy the If-Match parsing; 31 'insert returned no row' guards`, `Dead code and stale notes: worker.ts:339-342 enqueueJob unused; packages/db createDbCluster and lib/jwt.ts publicJwks never called; jobs/index.ts:44-46 placeholder handler; service/jobs.ts:15 comment says SLA_BREACH_CHECK is 'not yet part of the contracts JOB_NAMES enum' although enums.ts:792 has it; admin/tools.ts reseed summary says '501 until the seed module ships it' though seed exists`, `Positives: zero TODO/FIXME/HACK, zero console.log outside seed/migrate CLIs, zero 'as any', 0 import cycles across 140 files, reusable helpers (lib/routes.ts route(), lib/list.ts paginate(), lib/version.ts applyUpdate(), masters/factory.ts)`, `grep counts over apps/api/src: lines>200 chars 937, >300 chars 151, 'as unknown as' 129, 'req.user!' 232, 'returned no row' 28`

### A10. Type safety & validation — 810/1000 (v1: 540)

**Strengths.** End-to-end TypeScript with the strictest useful compiler flags, one Zod contract package consumed by API and all three front ends, strict request/query objects (unknown keys are 400), compiled response serialisation that fails closed, exhaustive enum label maps and Drizzle-inferred row types — a schema change breaks the build in every surface as intended.

**Gaps.** The seams where strings enter the system are untyped: ApiError codes, route permission keys and job names are plain strings or casts, which is exactly where the registry drift occurred. Report export code is dict-driven (Row = Record<string, unknown>) and reaches it through dozens of double casts, request context relies on 232 non-null assertions instead of a typed accessor, several response/details schemas are z.record/z.unknown/z.any, and per-code details schemas are not validated against what services actually send.

**Second auditor.** Confirmed: tsconfig.base.json strict + noUncheckedIndexedAccess + noImplicitOverride + verbatimModuleSyntax; typecheck recorded green; route() makes plain-object query schemas strict (routes.ts:70-72); response serialisation failures become 500 (errors.ts:70-74); contracts tests (182) pass locally. The untyped seams are exactly where drift occurred and are verified: ApiError.code is string (errors.ts:7), FastifyContextConfig.permission is string (idempotency.ts:9), two job names are cast 'as JobName' (service/jobs.ts:15, portal/files.ts:26), 129 'as unknown as' and 232 'req.user!' non-null assertions despite a typed staff(req) accessor existing (admin/common.ts:29) that only 9 call sites use, backup download responds z.any(), and BucketCapDetailsSchema does not match the payload. I shave 10 points because the typed accessor exists and is ignored across 232 sites, and because ERROR_CODES/JOB_NAMES/Permission unions are never asserted against runtime registries in any test.

**World-best would.** Type ApiError.code, permissions and job names as the contract unions, replace req.user! with a typed staff(req) accessor, model report rows and error details with concrete Zod schemas validated in tests, and eliminate z.any from response maps.

Evidence: `tsconfig.base.json: strict, noUncheckedIndexedAccess, noImplicitOverride, verbatimModuleSyntax; pnpm typecheck green for all 7 packages`, `packages/contracts: 179 z.strictObject request schemas vs 397 z.object (responses); lib/routes.ts:168-170 makes every query schema strict; errors.ts:66-69 serialises Zod issues; isResponseSerializationError -> 500 so a response that violates its schema never leaks`, `apps/api/src/lib/errors.ts:7 ApiError.code: string and plugins/idempotency.ts:9 FastifyContextConfig.permission?: string — the ErrorCode and Permission unions are not enforced at the throw/registration sites (18 unregistered error codes emitted, see factor 5)`, `129 'as unknown as' casts in apps/api/src (35 in reports/routes/reports.ts casting typed report rows to Row = Record<string, unknown> for dict-driven CSV/PDF export); 232 'req.user!' non-null assertions; service/jobs.ts:15 and portal/files.ts:26 'as JobName'`, `packages/contracts: 23 z.record and 17 z.unknown sites (cms section bodies, lead/booking activity payloads, audit before/after); admin/backup.ts:423 response: { 200: z.any() }; errors.ts:126-132 BucketCapDetailsSchema not matched by the payload at money/services/payments.ts:41`, `packages/contracts/test (182 tests incl. 123 adversarial) exercise canonical phone/PAN/money parsing; enums use enumSchema with LabelMap exhaustiveness`

## Group B — Database & Data Handling — **7970 / 10,000** (previous implementation: 4920)

| # | Factor | Previous (v1) | First score | Final score |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Data model fit: document store vs relational for a financial ledger | 480 | 900 | **870** |
| 2 | Referential integrity & constraints | 500 | 850 | **840** |
| 3 | Indexing strategy & query efficiency | 420 | 780 | **760** |
| 4 | Money representation & rounding | 630 | 910 | **900** |
| 5 | Canonical forms / normalisation | 590 | 850 | **830** |
| 6 | Snapshotting & versioning of structural data | 600 | 860 | **840** |
| 7 | Migrations & schema evolution | 450 | 770 | **720** |
| 8 | Soft delete / audit immutability | 530 | 860 | **830** |
| 9 | Backup, restore, right-to-erasure | 350 | 720 | **660** |
| 10 | Multi-tenancy readiness | 370 | 750 | **720** |

### B1. Data model fit: document store vs relational for a financial ledger — 870/1000 (v1: 480)

**Strengths.** The ledger lives in PostgreSQL 16 with real foreign keys, partial unique indexes that make double-raising and double-booking impossible under concurrency, enum types sourced from contracts, transactions plus per-booking advisory locks, and reporting done in SQL rather than aggregation pipelines. jsonb is confined to Zod-typed snapshots and small whole-read lists, and a db test suite pins the column-type conventions.

**Gaps.** Money still leaks into jsonb in a few places (bookings.flat_snapshot.price_paise and price_breakup, bookings.cancellation.refund_paise/deduction_paise, flats.price_breakup) where no bigint type or CHECK can protect it. Structural references are stored as uuid[] arrays (projects/blocks.stage_sequence, flat_types.spec_item_ids) with no FK, so integrity there depends on application usage checks. Running balances are duplicated on every ledger row and materialised on bookings, reconciled by application code (nightly integrity, recompute) rather than by any database-enforced invariant.

**Second auditor.** Re-verified: paise/percent/sqft helpers (packages/db/src/schema/_common.ts:40-47), partial unique indexes and the ledger UNIQUE(booking_id, seq) (money.ts:57-63,166), one-ACTIVE-booking-per-flat (bookings.ts:~95), advisory lock + atomic sequence (client.ts:138-150), and 50 green db tests in my own run. The relational design is sound and the first auditor's gaps are all real: money sits in jsonb (json.ts:113-116 PriceBreakupLine.amount_paise, :149 FlatSnapshot.price_paise, :181-182 Cancellation deduction/refund), stage sequences and spec items are uuid[] with no FK (inventory.ts:76,201; masters.ts:59), and there is no CHECK that demand cash+account = total or booking cash+account = agreement — those identities live only in Zod refinements (packages/contracts/src/modules/bookings.ts:66,242). Two things the first auditor under-weighted: ledger_entries carries both a UNIQUE and a duplicate btree index on the same columns (money.ts:166), and the ledger's running balances are not just 'duplicated' but can be wiped by any code path because the table is intentionally unguarded (0001_triggers.sql:24-26). 900 is slightly generous for a ledger whose core money identities the database cannot refuse; 870.

**World-best would.** Keep every money figure in typed bigint columns (a price_breakup_lines table, cancellation columns), model stage sequences and spec items as join tables with FKs, and add CHECK/trigger-level invariants (cash+account = agreement, demand total = cash+account) so the database itself refuses an inconsistent ledger.

Evidence: `packages/db/src/schema/_common.ts:40-47 — paise = bigint, percent = numeric(5,2), sqft = numeric(10,2) helpers used by every table`, `packages/db/src/schema/money.ts:57-63,166 — demands partial unique (booking_id, installment_seq) WHERE status <> 'VOID', compound indexes, ledger UNIQUE(booking_id, seq)`, `packages/db/src/schema/bookings.ts:117-121 — partial unique one ACTIVE booking per flat; bookings.ts:159-200 append-only agreement/plan revision tables`, `packages/db/src/client.ts:138-150 — pg_advisory_xact_lock helper and atomic INSERT … ON CONFLICT sequence counter`, `apps/api/src/modules/reports/services/moneyReports.ts:52,103,133 — reports are plain SQL joins filtered by company + project scope`, `packages/db/test/data-model.test.ts:89-125 — tests enforce bigint for every *_paise column, numeric(5,2) percents, date vs timestamptz (50 db tests green in my run)`

### B2. Referential integrity & constraints — 840/1000 (v1: 500)

**Strengths.** Nearly every relationship is a real FK, cascades are restricted to child rows that cannot exist alone, five business invariants are partial unique indexes enforced under concurrency, and all 16 master kinds carry usage queries that block deactivation with a structured 409 listing where the record is used (SPEC §2). FK and unique violations are mapped to proper API errors.

**Gaps.** Deliberate FK gaps remain: flats.booking_id and parking_slots.booking_id (inventory.ts:287,316), leads.booking_id (leads.ts:35), flats.rate_card_id, and the polymorphic documents.entity_id / notifications.entity_id have no FK, so orphans are only swept for notifications. Flats are hard-deleted on block regeneration (inventory/blocks.ts:255,298), which can orphan FLAT documents and notifications. There is no DB CHECK that cash_agreed + account_agreed = agreement_value, that a demand's cash+account = total, or that super ≥ builtup ≥ carpet (service-only, kinds.ts:224). Customer phone/PAN uniqueness is an advisory lock plus SELECT (crm/customerService.ts:203-227) rather than a unique index, so a raw-SQL or seed path can still create duplicates.

**Second auditor.** Verified by grep of the committed migration: 152 FOREIGN KEY constraints and 21 ON DELETE cascade, all on genuine child rows (co-applicants, other charges, plan installments, block_stages, flats→blocks, scopes, lead children, ticket messages, presets). Deactivate blocks on usage with a 409 IN_USE payload (masters/factory.ts:172-178) and every kind carries usage queries (kinds.ts:83-463, jsonb containment backed by GIN from drizzle/0001_central_fixes.sql). 23505/23503 are translated (lib/pgErrors.ts:26-27). The deliberate FK gaps are confirmed in code: flats.booking_id and parking_slots.booking_id (inventory.ts:~287,~316), leads.booking_id (leads.ts:35), flats.rate_card_id, documents.entity_id (documents.ts:17) and notifications.entity_id (service.ts:83) are bare uuids. Customer phone/PAN uniqueness is an advisory lock plus SELECT (customerService.ts:203-227) over non-unique indexes (customers.ts:62-63), and no CHECK exists for the money identities. One additional hazard: flats.block_id is ON DELETE cascade, so a block delete would cascade flats; only bookings.flat_id (no action) stops a booked flat from vanishing, and FLAT documents/notifications are polymorphic and would orphan. The first auditor's 850 is fair; I shave 10 for the unguarded cascade path and because the only concurrency test for duplicate identities covers leads, not customers (crm-adversarial.test.ts:136).

**World-best would.** Replace polymorphic/no-FK uuid columns with FK-bearing columns or link tables (bookings ↔ flats via a deferrable FK), add CHECK constraints for the money identities, and add company-scoped unique indexes on customers(company_id, phone) and (company_id, pan_hash) WHERE erased_at IS NULL so the database is the last line of defence.

Evidence: `packages/db/drizzle/0000_initial.sql — 152 FOREIGN KEY constraints, 21 ON DELETE cascade limited to pure child rows (installments, stage snapshots, co-applicants, scopes)`, `packages/db/src/sql/0001_triggers.sql:71-85 and packages/db/src/schema/bookings.ts:36 — five partial unique invariants plus UNIQUE NULLS NOT DISTINCT on plan assignments; packages/db/test/invariants.test.ts (10 tests green)`, `apps/api/src/modules/masters/factory.ts:172-178 — deactivate returns 409 IN_USE with usage_count/used_by when any usage line is > 0`, `apps/api/src/modules/masters/kinds.ts:289-293,398-401,434,464-467 — usage queries per master (flats, block_stages, plan installments, payments, escrow accounts) incl. jsonb containment backed by GIN indexes from drizzle/0001_central_fixes.sql`, `apps/api/src/lib/pgErrors.ts:26-27 — 23505/23503 translated to 409 DUPLICATE / 400 instead of 500`, `packages/db/src/schema/money.ts:120 and leads.ts:179 — CHECK amount_paise > 0, visitors 1..6, rating 1..5`

### B3. Indexing strategy & query efficiency — 760/1000 (v1: 420)

**Strengths.** Every list endpoint's filter+sort shape has a compound index that a test checks against the data model, pagination is keyset-capable with a correct microsecond tiebreaker, counts switch to planner estimates above 10k rows, and the measured p95 at 200 hammering connections is under 300 ms for every list and detail page except the 25-row bookings page (404 ms). The profiling write-up is honest and the fixes (prepared statements, single JSON-aggregating related query, UNION search with cancel) are in code.

**Gaps.** Text search is ILIKE '%term%' over customers.full_name/email (crm/customers.ts:146), leads (crm/leads.ts:138) and projects/blocks (search/index.ts) with no pg_trgm GIN index, and the prefix ILIKE on phone/code cannot use the plain btree indexes (no text_pattern_ops / citext), so at lakh-row customer tables these become sequential scans — the 111 ms customers-search number was measured on 12 customers. ledger_entries_booking_seq_idx duplicates the unique constraint on the same columns (money.ts:166), costing writes for nothing. ARCHITECTURE.md §6 claims monthly partitioning scripts in packages/db/partitions, but that folder does not exist. At 1,000 zero-think-time connections list pages miss the 300 ms target (0.5–1.7 s), and the estimated-count path has never been exercised on real volumes.

**Second auditor.** Verified paginate(): one page query + one count query, keyset cursor with a microsecond text key, allow-listed sort, planner-estimate counts above 10k rows with a 60 s pg_class cache (lib/list.ts:103-190); prepared statements are forced on every Drizzle query (client.ts:40-70); search is one UNION ALL with server-side cancel (search/index.ts:30-70). The performance tables are genuine and honest (docs/PERFORMANCE.md: 200 conns p95 102-209 ms for lists, bookings 404 ms; 1,000 conns 0.5-1.7 s). But the gaps are more serious than the score implies for a 5-lakh-customer builder: every text search is ILIKE '%term%' on full_name/email (crm/customers.ts:146; search/index.ts:44) and ILIKE 'term%' on phone/code, and grep finds no pg_trgm, text_pattern_ops or tsvector anywhere in packages, apps/api or docs — the (company_id, full_name) btree cannot serve either pattern, so these are sequential scans per company at scale, and the 111 ms customer-search figure was measured on 12 customers (PERFORMANCE.md 'How the numbers are produced'). ARCHITECTURE.md:103 promises monthly partitioning scripts in packages/db/partitions; the folder does not exist. The ledger carries a redundant index (money.ts:166). The estimated-count path has never run against real volumes. 760.

**World-best would.** Add pg_trgm GIN indexes (or a tsvector column) for every ILIKE search column and text_pattern_ops indexes for prefix searches, drop the redundant ledger index, ship the partitioning scripts the docs promise, and load-test against a synthetic 5-lakh-customer / 1-crore-ledger-row dataset so index choices are validated at scale rather than on seed data.

Evidence: `apps/api/src/lib/list.ts:150-190 — paginate(): one page query + one count query, keyset cursors with microsecond-exact timestamp keys, allow-listed sort`, `apps/api/src/lib/list.ts:103-140 — total via EXPLAIN planner estimate above 10k rows (pg_class reltuples cached 60 s) unless exact=true`, `packages/db/src/client.ts:40-70 — every Drizzle query runs as a named prepared statement (planning 1.3 ms → cached)`, `apps/api/src/modules/search/index.ts:30-46,65 — command-palette search is one UNION ALL statement, ILIKE contains on names / prefix on codes, cancelled server-side on timeout`, `packages/db/test/data-model.test.ts:51 — every index listed in DATA-MODEL.md verified by column list; docs/PERFORMANCE.md 'Not changed' note: EXPLAIN ANALYZE execution 0.1–0.3 ms on seed data`, `docs/PERFORMANCE.md run 2026-10-25T20:42 (200 conns, 4 workers): projects p95 102 ms, flats 209 ms, customers search 111 ms, bookings list 404 ms; run 20:49 (1,000 conns): lists p95 493–1,731 ms`

### B4. Money representation & rounding — 900/1000 (v1: 630)

**Strengths.** Money is integer paise end to end: bigint columns, int-only Zod schemas on every wire input, BigInt half-up rounding, basis-point percentages, and a telescoping installment engine that is unit-tested (including a property test) and reproduced by an integration test for the ₹1 Cr worked example. Agreement revisions and the ledger rebuild use the same rounding functions, so stored and recomputed figures agree.

**Gaps.** bigint columns are read in JS number mode (client.ts:118) and raw SQL sums (payments.ts:24-33) go through Number(), so anything above 2^53 paise (~₹90 lakh crore) would silently lose precision rather than fail — unrealistic for one builder but not bank-grade. hasMaxDecimals uses a 1e-6 float tolerance (common.ts:31-34), so 12.3450000001 passes as two decimals. rate_card_plc_rules.value is numeric(14,4) read as a JS float (inventory.ts:362, service.ts:238) and a stored 4-dp paise value would make assertPaise throw a 500 instead of a 422 (only the contract refine prevents it). Percent-of-agreed arithmetic is exact, but raised_percent is summed as numeric(5,2) via SQL and demands.percent is a float on the wire, so a plan with more than two decimals is unrepresentable by design rather than rejected with a clear message.

**Second auditor.** Verified end to end: divRoundHalfUp on BigInt, digit-wise scaleDecimal, basis-point pctOfPaise/bpOfPaise (packages/domain/src/money.ts:35-150), telescoping installmentAmounts (dues.ts:163-190), int-only PaiseSchema and ≤2-dp PercentSchema (contracts/common.ts:16-43), bigint columns, and demandAmountsFor/agreement revision reusing the same functions (ledger.ts:120-128; bookings.ts:191-205). 124 domain and 182 contracts tests passed in my run. The driver boundary is the weak point exactly as described: client.ts:118 parses bigint with Number(), and I confirmed Number('9007199254740993') → 9007199254740992, so an out-of-range value would be silently corrupted rather than refused (raw SQL sums in payments.ts:24-33 go through the same Number()). One correction to the first auditor: hasMaxDecimals rejects 12.3450000001 (I evaluated it: false) but accepts 12.340000001 (true), after which the domain's toBasisPoints throws MoneyError for >2 decimals — a float can still slip past the contract and surface as an engine error instead of a validation error. rate_card_plc_rules.value is a numeric(14,4) float carrying paise or percent depending on calc_mode (inventory.ts:~362). These are edge-case hygiene issues, not correctness bugs on realistic inputs; 900.

**World-best would.** Carry paise as BigInt (or string) across the driver boundary and reject anything outside a defined range, store PLC paise values in a bigint column with a separate percent column, and express percents on the wire as strings or basis points so no float ever touches a money path.
