Evidence: `apps/api/src/lib/routes.ts:126-128 — route() throws at registration unless exactly one of permission | audience | public is declared; apps/api/test/integration/rbac.test.ts:41-56 — registry test fails on any staff route without a permission (docs/generated/api_map.md does not exist; the live registry at GET /api/v1/system/routes replaces it)`, `apps/api/src/plugins/auth.ts:118-126,145-148 — audience checked before identity load (staff token on portal route ⇒ 401 INVALID_AUDIENCE), permission alternatives 'a|b' enforced per route`, `apps/api/src/lib/scope.ts:203-213 — scopeFilter() yields false for a scoped user with no projects; ensureProjectAccess() throws 404 never 403; apps/api/src/modules/portal/common.ts:37-39,74-80,102-107 — primary-or-co-applicant ownership condition, 404 otherwise, synthetic permission-less reader for reused services`, `apps/api/src/modules/search/index.ts:44-47,67 — GET /search is guarded only by project.read yet its CUSTOMER branch returns full_name, customer_code and phone and its BOOKING branch booking numbers with no customer.read/booking.read check; packages/contracts/src/permissions.ts:217,285-292 — Employee and Marketing roles hold project.read but not customer.read/booking.read`, `apps/api/src/modules/users/index.ts:148-176 — PATCH /users/:id lets a users.manage holder change their own role_id to any role (no self-escalation or permission-superset guard); apps/api/src/modules/roles/index.ts:101 — custom roles may carry any permission set`, `PoC: POST /roles {permissions:[dashboard.view,users.manage]} → user 'mgr' → PATCH /users/{self} {role_id:<Super Admin>} → 200, role_name 'Super Admin'; GET /auth/me permissions.length 45`

### D3. Field-level encryption, masking, audited reveal — 770/1000 (v1: 720)

**Strengths.** Authenticated encryption with random IVs, keyed hashes for duplicate detection, masking by default in every list/detail/CSV, reveal gated by a dedicated permission and audited per request, MFA secrets and company bank accounts under the same scheme, and backups that strip secrets by default; the no-leak test (security.test.ts:247-278) greps every response and audit row for plaintext secrets.

**Gaps.** Rotation is a format promise, not a feature: FIELD_KEY_ID is a constant, decryptField rejects any other kid, and SECURITY.md's 'key ids allow rotation without downtime' is not implemented. Keys are SHA-256 derivations of environment strings with no KMS/HSM or envelope encryption, and ciphertexts carry no AAD, so a ciphertext can be transplanted between rows/columns undetected. Masking decrypts every row on every list even though pan_last4/aadhaar_last4 are stored, widening key usage for no benefit. Audit snapshots retain customer PII in clear after erasure, which undermines the DPDP right-to-erasure story.

**Second auditor.** Verified: crypto.ts:15-33 is AES-256-GCM with a random 12-byte IV and a `k1.` prefix, keyedHash is HMAC-SHA256 under a separately derived key, customerService.ts:26-40 decrypts in-process and returns only masked values, customers.ts:200 writes `customer.kyc_reveal` in the same transaction, and the security no-leak test passed on my database. The gaps are confirmed and slightly worse than scored: decryptField throws for any kid other than the constant 'k1' (crypto.ts:10,29), so the SECURITY.md:30 promise of 'key ids allow rotation without downtime' is false today; there is no AAD binding ciphertext to (table,column,row), so a ciphertext can be moved between customers undetected; keys are plain SHA-256 derivations of env strings with no KMS; and the API connects as a Postgres superuser (PoC: `select rolsuper from pg_roles where rolname=current_user` → true), so the column-level encryption is the only barrier between an application compromise and every ciphertext plus the key in the same process. Masking decrypts every row on every list even though pan_last4/account_last4 are stored. Audit snapshots keep plaintext names/phones forever after erasure (erase.ts:1-5). Solid but a notch below the first score because the rotation claim in the docs is unimplemented and there is no AAD.

**World-best would.** Envelope-encrypt with per-record data keys wrapped by a KMS key, bind AAD (table, column, row id), support multiple key ids with background re-encryption, mask from stored last4 without decrypting, and tokenise or crypto-shred PII inside audit snapshots on erasure.

Evidence: `apps/api/src/lib/crypto.ts:9-37 — AES-256-GCM with 12-byte random IV, k1.<iv>.<tag>.<ct> format, HMAC-SHA256 keyed hash under a separately derived key; decryptField() throws for any kid other than the hard-coded FIELD_KEY_ID (no multi-key rotation)`, `apps/api/src/modules/crm/customerService.ts:52-63,76-90,171-178 — PAN/Aadhaar/bank stored as ciphertext + keyed hash + last4; masked views decrypt each row to mask it; revealedRow() returns plaintext only after the caller audited`, `apps/api/src/modules/crm/customers.ts:189-203 — ?reveal=true requires customer.kyc_view (assertPermission ⇒ 403) and writes a customer.kyc_reveal audit row in the same transaction; apps/api/test/integration/crm-adversarial.test.ts:171,274 — reveal logged exactly once, co-applicants ciphertext at rest`, `apps/api/src/modules/documents/routes.ts:181-199 — sensitive (KYC) document links need customer.kyc_view and write document.sensitive_link audit rows; apps/api/src/modules/admin/backup.ts:35-36 — *_enc, password_hash, mfa_*, token_hash, pan/aadhaar hashes stripped from exports unless include_sensitive`, `apps/api/src/modules/admin/erase.ts:1-5 — erasure anonymises PII but 'never touches audit history', so before/after snapshots keep plaintext names/phones forever`, `apps/api/src/lib/crypto.ts:10-12,28-29 — FIELD_KEY_ID='k1'; decryptField: if (kid !== FIELD_KEY_ID …) throw`

### D4. Input validation & injection safety — 820/1000 (v1: 680)

**Strengths.** Zod contracts on every body/query/param with strict objects, Drizzle parameterisation everywhere, explicit LIKE escaping, allow-listed sort fields, bounded string lengths, Fastify's secure JSON parsing, no user-derived paths, and adversarial tests for filename/header injection, unknown keys and over-large pages. Operator-injection via query strings does not apply to this Postgres/Zod stack.

**Gaps.** CSV exports are vulnerable to spreadsheet formula injection from attacker-controlled fields (website enquiry names, customer notes). No control-character or zero-width/bidi stripping on names and free text (NameSchema only trims). The global search bypasses Drizzle with sql.unsafe, which is safe today but is the one place a future edit could introduce interpolation. No central HTML sanitisation of CMS markdown is evidenced in the API (rendering is left to clients).

**Second auditor.** Verified the positives: routes.ts:72-73,92 makes every plain-object query schema strict, the only sql.unsafe in the API (search/index.ts:53) binds $1..$n with a server-computed `per` and a 150 ms cancel budget, LIKE terms go through escapeLike, filenames are stripped of path/control/quote characters (documents/service.ts:203-212) and the ERP ships a custom markdown renderer that treats raw HTML as text and allow-lists URL schemes (apps/erp/src/features/cms/markdown.ts:3-4,38) — a mitigation the first auditor did not credit. The CSV formula injection is confirmed by PoC: an unauthenticated POST /public/enquiries with name `=HYPERLINK("http://evil","x")` landed in the admin's GET /leads?format=csv as `"=HYPERLINK(""http://evil"",""x"")"` — RFC-4180-quoted but still a live formula when opened in Excel/Sheets (csv.ts:44-48 has no leading-character neutralisation). NameSchema is only trim/min/max (contracts/common.ts:142), so zero-width/bidi/C0 characters pass into names, PDFs and CSVs. Score essentially stands: strong validation discipline with one concrete, externally triggerable injection.

**World-best would.** Prefix dangerous leading characters in CSV cells, strip C0/C1/zero-width characters in a shared string preprocessor, keep every SQL statement in the query builder, and fuzz the contracts (property-based tests) against the validators.

Evidence: `apps/api/src/lib/routes.ts:110-113,131 — every plain-object querystring schema is made strict (unknown keys ⇒ 400); packages/contracts/src/common.ts:141-154,187-203 — bounded names/text, SortSchema regex allow-list, search ≤ 100 chars, page_size ≤ 200`, `apps/api/src/lib/list.ts:67-77 — escapeLike() escapes \ % _ before ILIKE; all queries go through Drizzle parameters (grep for sql.raw in apps/api/src returns nothing)`, `apps/api/src/modules/search/index.ts:25-27,52-62 — the one sql.unsafe statement binds $1..$n parameters, per is server-computed, and the query is cancelled server-side after 150 ms`, `apps/api/src/modules/documents/service.ts:203-212 and routes.ts:212,227-230 — filenames stripped of path/control/quote characters, header-safe Content-Disposition; apps/api/src/app.ts:53,83 — 2 MB JSON body cap, 15 MB multipart cap`, `apps/api/src/lib/csv.ts:43-48 — csvField() quotes commas/quotes/newlines but never neutralises a leading = + - @, so a public enquiry name like =HYPERLINK(...) (publicForms.ts:88-110) lands in the leads CSV export as a live formula`, `PoC: public enquiry name '=HYPERLINK("http://evil","x")' → 200; GET /api/v1/leads?format=csv contains LD-2627/0001,"=HYPERLINK(""http://evil"",""x"")",9876501234,…`

### D5. Rate limiting & abuse protection — 760/1000 (v1: 610)

**Strengths.** Cheap, correct O(1) Redis limiter (no database-backed counters), separate budgets for login, refresh, forms, public reads and per-user writes, honeypot handling that is indistinguishable from a real submission, load shedding, image-bomb and search-budget guards, and tests for the 6th-request-429 behaviour on login, portal login and forms.

**Gaps.** Per-IP identity is only as good as the proxy: taking the first XFF hop with TRUST_PROXY=true by default is spoofable in the ALB/NGINX topology the deploy doc recommends (the shipped Caddy config happens to overwrite the header). Authenticated reads have no per-user ceiling, so one leaked token can hammer list endpoints without limit. Account lockout is itself a denial-of-service lever (anyone can lock a known staff e-mail every 15 minutes). The declared `rateLimit.by: 'subject'` option is never implemented, there is no CAPTCHA/proof-of-work escalation for forms, and Redis unavailability turns into a hard outage rather than a documented degrade.

**Second auditor.** The limiter itself is correct and cheap (rateLimit.ts:9-25: one MULTI, two weighted fixed windows, 429 + Retry-After) and the per-route budgets exist as cited (login/mfa 10/min, refresh 60/min, forms 5/min, public 300/min, writes 120/min/user). The weaknesses are confirmed in code: `rateLimit.by: 'subject'` is declared in RouteDef (routes.ts:36) but auth.ts:113-116 only ever buckets public routes by IP; clientIp() takes the FIRST X-Forwarded-For hop whenever TRUST_PROXY=true, which is the default (config.ts:44, rateLimit.ts:31-37) and Fastify's own trustProxy is set to the same boolean (app.ts:54), so any deployment where the API is reachable without a header-rewriting proxy, or behind an appending ALB/NGINX as DEPLOY.md:32 recommends, lets a client choose its own rate-limit identity; the shipped Caddy config is safe only because Caddy replaces XFF from untrusted clients by default. The per-account lockout is a remote DoS lever (PoC above: five bad passwords from five IPs locked the real user). Both `app.redis.mget` in loadStaff/loadPortal (auth.ts:72,92) and the limiter's MULTI are unwrapped, so a Redis outage is a total API outage, not a degrade. Authenticated GETs have no per-user ceiling. Slightly lower than the first score because the XFF default plus the lockout lever are both exploitable without any special position.

**World-best would.** Resolve the client IP from the right-most untrusted hop with a configured trusted-proxy list, add per-user read budgets and per-account login throttles distinct from lockout, escalate to CAPTCHA on abuse signals, and define fail-open/closed behaviour per limiter when Redis is down.

Evidence: `apps/api/src/lib/rateLimit.ts:9-25 — Redis sliding-window (two weighted fixed windows) in one MULTI, multi-instance safe, 429 + Retry-After; apps/api/src/plugins/auth.ts:113-116,149,159 — 300/min/IP for public routes, 120/min per user for staff/portal writes`, `apps/api/src/modules/auth/staff.ts:82,107,121 and portal.ts:49,86 — login 10/min/IP, refresh 60/min/IP; apps/api/src/modules/web/publicForms.ts:22,88-105 and shared.ts:41-60 — forms 5/min/IP, honeypot replies with a believable fake number so bots cannot tell`, `apps/api/src/lib/rateLimit.ts:31-39 + apps/api/src/config.ts:136 — TRUST_PROXY defaults to true and the FIRST X-Forwarded-For hop is used; apps/api/test/integration/web-adversarial.test.ts:205-213 asserts this, so behind an appending proxy (NGINX/ALB, named in docs/DEPLOY.md:32) or with the API exposed directly, a client can spoof its way past every per-IP limit`, `apps/api/src/plugins/loadShed.ts:11-28 and app.ts:74-82 — in-flight cap with 503 + Retry-After and @fastify/under-pressure; documents/service.ts:33,170-185 — 50 MP image-bomb guard; search/index.ts:9 — 150 ms query budget`, `apps/api/src/lib/rateLimit.ts:15 and plugins/auth.ts:72 — a Redis outage makes the limiter and the auth cache throw, so every public and authenticated request fails 500 (no fail-open/closed policy)`, `apps/api/src/lib/routes.ts:36 vs apps/api/src/plugins/auth.ts:113-116 — by is never read; bucket is always pub:<url|all>:<ip>`

### D6. Secrets & key management — 660/1000 (v1: 520)

**Strengths.** Secrets come only from the environment, production refuses to boot without them, purpose-specific key derivation keeps JWT, link, field and hash keys separate, tokens are stored hashed, CI uses its own throw-away secrets, and the idempotency layer never trusts an unverified token.

**Gaps.** There is no working rotation path for either the signing key or the field-encryption key despite the documentation claiming one. Weak placeholder secrets from .env.example pass validation in production and nothing rejects known-default values. Staff temporary passwords are written in clear to the idempotency store, demo credentials are seeded by default, S3 credentials are plain env vars, and there is no KMS/secret-manager integration, no secret scanning in CI (.github/workflows/ci.yml), and no documented key custody or rotation runbook.

**Second auditor.** Partly refuting the first auditor in the product's favour: the .env.example FIELD_ENCRYPTION_KEY placeholder is 25 characters and fails the ≥32 check (config.ts:14), so a verbatim copy of .env.example will NOT boot in production — only the JWT_SECRET (37 chars) and HASH_KEY (18 chars) placeholders would pass, which still means a half-edited env can run with a guessable signing key; and backup.ts:37-38 does strip idempotency_keys.response_body from non-sensitive exports, which the first auditor did not credit. Everything else holds or is worse: both JWT `kid` and the field key id are the constant 'k1' and verification/decryption accept only that id (jwt.ts:33,43-46; crypto.ts:10,29), contradicting SECURITY.md:30 and .env.example:8 ('Rotate by adding a new key id'); keys are SHA-256(context‖secret) with no KMS; the idempotency layer is sound (scope = verified req.subjectId, idempotency.ts:75) but POST /users (users/index.ts:113-137, idempotent: true) persists temp_password in Redis and idempotency_keys for 24 h; the database role is a superuser (PoC) and the seed code itself relies on that by issuing `set local session_replication_role = replica` (seed/run.ts:116); CI (ci.yml) has no secret scanning or dependency audit; and demo credentials are seeded by default in the production compose file. No rotation runbook, no deny-list for placeholder secrets, no secret manager. Fragile-to-adequate.

**World-best would.** Load keys from a KMS/secret manager with versioned key ids, keep two signing keys live during rotation (JWKS), re-encrypt fields in the background on key change, deny-list placeholder/default secrets at boot, exclude one-time secrets from idempotent replay, and run secret scanning in CI.

Evidence: `apps/api/src/config.ts:105-107,149-166 — Zod env schema requires JWT_SECRET ≥ 32, FIELD_ENCRYPTION_KEY ≥ 32, HASH_KEY ≥ 16; insecure defaults are injected only when NODE_ENV !== production`, `apps/api/src/plugins/idempotency.ts:250-260 — the idempotency scope uses req.subjectId set by the auth onRequest hook after full signature verification; no unverified JWT decoding anywhere`, `apps/api/src/lib/jwt.ts:18-27,33 and lib/crypto.ts:9-12,28 — all keys are SHA-256 derivations of three env strings with context labels; JWT kid and field key id are both the constant 'k1' and verification/decryption accept only that id, contradicting docs/SECURITY.md:30 ('key ids allow rotation without downtime')`, `.env.example:8-12 — the placeholder JWT_SECRET (36 chars) and HASH_KEY (18 chars) satisfy the length checks, so copying the example file into production starts the API with guessable signing and hashing keys`, `apps/api/src/modules/users/index.ts:113-137 + plugins/idempotency.ts:289-295 — POST /users is idempotent and returns temp_password, so the one-time password is persisted in Redis and idempotency_keys for 24 h (crm/portalAccess.ts:100 deliberately avoids this for customers, users does not)`, `apps/api/src/server.ts:156-158 + seed/bootstrap.ts:66-73 — SEED_ON_BOOT=true by default seeds six documented passwords (docs/SEED.md:6-18) in any environment`

### D7. Audit log completeness & immutability — 790/1000 (v1: 630)

**Strengths.** Immutability is enforced in the database for UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE, every write path goes through one helper inside the same transaction with before/after snapshots and request correlation, portal and job actors are distinguished, reveals and sensitive downloads are audited, and the read-only audit UI/CSV plus login-event history give investigators what they need.

**Gaps.** Completeness rests on convention: only the CRM module has a test that every mutating route writes an audit row, so the ~470-endpoint surface is not verified mechanically. Audit snapshots keep customer PII that the erasure flow explicitly leaves untouched. There is no tamper-evidence (hash chain, signed batches) or off-box WORM shipping, and because the API connects as the database owner the triggers are a convention a compromised app or operator can disable. No retention/archival policy or partitioning for a table that grows with every write.

**Second auditor.** Verified: 0001_triggers.sql:8-23 installs BEFORE UPDATE/DELETE row triggers and a BEFORE TRUNCATE statement trigger on audit_logs; writeAudit (audit.ts:51-61) runs inside the caller's transaction and strips sensitive keys with a reasonable regex (audit.ts:26); the audit module exposes only GET routes and no code path in apps/api/src updates or deletes auditLogs (grep for .delete(auditLogs/.update(auditLogs is empty); reseed keeps audit_logs (run.ts:57). The weakening point is operational rather than code: the application connects as a Postgres superuser (PoC rolsuper=true) and the codebase itself demonstrates the bypass (seed/run.ts:116 sets session_replication_role=replica, which disables these triggers), so 'immutable' holds only against SQL that forgets to disable triggers. There is no hash chain, signing or off-box shipping, no retention/partitioning, and completeness is enforced by one CRM test rather than a registry-driven test over all ~396 staff routes. Login events with company_id NULL are shown to every tenant's audit.view (audit/index.ts:51). Still clearly production-usable; marked down a little for the superuser reality and lack of tamper evidence.

**World-best would.** Generate a registry-driven test asserting an audit row for every non-GET route, hash-chain or periodically sign audit batches and ship them to an append-only external store, run the application under a role without trigger/DDL privileges, and tokenise PII inside snapshots so erasure can redact without breaking the chain.

Evidence: `packages/db/src/sql/0001_triggers.sql:4-23 — BEFORE UPDATE/DELETE row trigger and a statement-level TRUNCATE trigger raise on audit_logs; packages/db/src/schema/identity.ts:147-169 — append-only columns with entity/actor/time indexes`, `apps/api/src/lib/audit.ts:113-152 — writeAudit() runs inside the caller's transaction with actor, action, entity, before/after, ip, request id; sensitive keys stripped recursively (unit-tested in test/unit/audit.test.ts)`, `apps/api/src/modules/audit/index.ts:23-88 — the audit module exposes only GET routes (list, per-entity history, login events, CSV); no API path can modify audit rows; apps/api/src/seed/run.ts:57 — reseed keeps audit_logs`, `Per-module count (mutating routes vs writeAudit calls): auth 16/16, crm 21/23, money 21/22, service 8/12, construction 6/9, users 6/6, roles 3/3, documents 4/4, masters via factory.ts:64,79,179, portal mutations audited inside service functions (portal/profile.ts:112, service module); apps/api/test/integration/crm-adversarial.test.ts:553 asserts every CRM mutation is audited`, `apps/api/src/modules/admin/erase.ts:1-5 and apps/api/src/modules/admin/backup.ts:96 — erasure never redacts audit PII; restore instructions rely on SET session_replication_role = replica, i.e. the DB role the app uses can bypass the triggers`, `PoC: select rolsuper from pg_roles where rolname = current_user → true on the shipped connection string pattern`

### D8. Public API allow-listing / leakage — 860/1000 (v1: 690)

**Strengths.** Public output is built from explicit pick-lists rather than filtered rows, availability and pricing honour per-project toggles, media is reference-allow-listed, caching is keyed on validated input, the site can be fully dark behind a coming-soon flag, and adversarial tests check for leaked ids, costs, storage keys and hold/booked distinctions.

**Gaps.** The full OpenAPI document and Swagger UI are exposed unauthenticated in production, handing attackers a complete route and schema map. Availability grids still expose per-unit flat numbers, facing and counts that let a competitor infer sales velocity (arguably product intent, but not configurable). Public endpoints return absolute API-origin media URLs with document UUIDs, which are stable identifiers even if not internal row ids of people.

**Second auditor.** Verified publicContent.ts:210-247 (media served only while referenced by published CMS/stage/hero/OG/floor-plan/brand content, with siteIsLive checks), availabilityGrid (:255-266) emitting only toPublicAvailability() and a price only when show_prices, cache keys from validated input, and the adversarial tests. The one concrete leak is confirmed by PoC: GET /api/v1/openapi.json is `public: true` (system/index.ts:66-70) and returned 200 with 361 paths and every schema, and /api/v1/docs is a hard-coded public prefix (routes.ts:54) — in production that hands an attacker the exact route/permission/schema map including admin, backup and erase endpoints. Public media URLs expose document UUIDs and the API origin, and per-unit flat numbers/facings/counts are always exposed when the grid is on (no per-project toggle). Still the strongest factor in the group; trimmed slightly because the OpenAPI exposure is unconditional and un-flagged in the docs/TODO.

**World-best would.** Gate the OpenAPI/Swagger routes behind admin.tools or disable them in production, make per-unit detail an explicit per-project toggle, and add a contract-level test that every Public* schema is closed (strict) so a new field cannot leak by omission.

Evidence: `apps/api/src/modules/web/publicContent.ts:1-3,255-266 — allow-listed serialisers; availability cells carry only AVAILABLE | NOT_AVAILABLE and a price only when show_prices; packages/contracts/src/enums.ts:699-706 — toPublicAvailability() collapses HOLD/BLOCKED/BOOKED/SOLD`, `apps/api/src/modules/web/publicContent.ts:205-247,570-589 — /public/media/:id serves a document only while published content references it (CMS, website-flagged stage photos, hero/OG/floor plans, logo/favicon); unpublished, sensitive or KYC documents are 404 even by id`, `apps/api/src/modules/web/shared.ts:84-111 — public cache keys are built from validated params/query so junk query strings cannot grow Redis; not-found is cached too; shared.ts:41-60 — honeypot replies use the company's real numbering pattern`, `apps/api/test/integration/web-adversarial.test.ts:136,162,306 and docs/ACCEPTANCE.md:22 — tests assert 7 public endpoints carry no id/*_id/storage/cost keys and no HOLD/BOOKED/BLOCKED/SOLD, hidden media is 404, coming-soon hides everything but legal pages`, `apps/api/src/modules/system/index.ts:66-70 and app.ts:94 — /api/v1/openapi.json and the Swagger UI are public in every environment, enumerating all 471 routes and schemas`, `PoC: unauthenticated GET /api/v1/openapi.json → 200, Object.keys(paths).length = 361`

### D9. File upload safety — 820/1000 (v1: 570)

**Strengths.** Defence in depth from parser limits through byte-sniffing, strict allow-list, uuid keys, path-safe storage, decompression-bomb checks, orphan cleanup on failure, hardened download headers and short-lived constant-time signed links, with the portal variant bound to the customer; the test suite probes most classic upload attacks.

**Gaps.** Uploads are fully buffered in memory (part.toBuffer) so 15 MB × concurrency is a memory lever. No malware scanning and no PDF sanitisation, so JavaScript-bearing PDFs are stored and redistributed to staff and customers. Only the local-disk driver ships (createStorage throws for s3), so files have no server-side encryption, object lock or CDN offload, and are streamed through the API workers. Staff signed links are bearer-less (anyone holding the URL for 15 minutes can fetch a KYC scan), and there is no per-user upload quota.

**Second auditor.** Verified the chain: multipart limits (app.ts:83), detectMime via file-type with a PDF/JPEG/PNG/WebP allow-list (documents/service.ts:92-99), uuid storage keys never derived from the client name (:102-107), SAFE_KEY regex + '..' check in storage.ts:16-20, declared-dimension image-bomb guard (:170-185), hardened download headers (routes.ts:202-220) and canonical re-encoding + constant-time compare for link tokens (:244-262). Portal links are customer-bound (portal/files.ts:1-8). Confirmed gaps: uploads are fully buffered (multipart.ts:22 part.toBuffer) so 15 MB × concurrency is a memory lever with no per-user quota; JavaScript-bearing PDFs are accepted by design (documents.test.ts:482-489) and redistributed to customers; createStorage throws for s3 (storage.ts:46), so there is no SSE/object-lock/CDN path and every byte streams through API workers; and staff `/files/:token` is a pure bearer link (any holder, any IP, no session binding, 15 min, Cache-Control private max-age=900) — the sensitive_link audit row records who minted it, not who fetched it. Minor trim for the missing S3 driver that DEPLOY.md presents as a config switch.

**World-best would.** Stream uploads straight to object storage with size-limited multipart, scan with ClamAV/YARA and flatten PDFs before release, store under SSE-KMS with object lock, bind staff links to the requesting session, and serve media via a CDN with signed cookies.

Evidence: `apps/api/src/modules/documents/service.ts:91-99,113-116 — type sniffed from bytes with file-type, allow-list of PDF/JPEG/PNG/WebP (SVG rejected), 15 MB cap; app.ts:83 and lib/multipart.ts:49-72 — parser-level fileSize/parts limits with 413/400 mapping`, `apps/api/src/modules/documents/service.ts:101-107 and lib/storage.ts:16-20 — storage keys are company/yyyy/mm/uuid, client filename never used; key regex + '..' check before any path join`, `apps/api/src/modules/documents/service.ts:170-185,203-212 — image-bomb guard on declared dimensions before decoding, filename stripped of paths/control chars with honest extensions; routes.ts:202-220 — PDFs served as attachment, images inline, nosniff, CORP, exact Content-Length`, `apps/api/src/modules/documents/service.ts:228-263 and portal/files.ts:1-8 — 15-minute HMAC-SHA256 link tokens compared in constant time after canonical re-encoding; portal links additionally bound to the issuing customer and refused for any other bearer`, `apps/api/test/integration/documents.test.ts:172,482,495,527 — EXE/HTML-as-PDF rejected, SVG rejected but PDF with embedded JavaScript accepted, 100 MP bomb rejected, traversal/header-injection filenames neutralised`, `apps/api/src/lib/multipart.ts:22 — await part.toBuffer(); no streaming to storage`

### D10. Security headers, CORS — 700/1000 (v1: 610)

**Strengths.** Bearer-only auth with credentials-free CORS removes CSRF, the origin allow-list is explicit, the API sets conservative defaults (no-store, nosniff, HSTS, request ids), revocation is immediate across all instances via Redis rather than waiting for token expiry, and users can see and kill their sessions on both surfaces.

**Gaps.** No Content-Security-Policy on any surface and no frame-ancestors protection on the ERP or portal web hosts, so the staff SPA is clickjackable and any XSS can read the localStorage refresh token and keep a session alive for seven days. Refresh tokens are not bound to a device or held in an httpOnly cookie, and there is no option to do so. The Swagger UI is public, the portal host lacks Referrer-Policy/Permissions-Policy, and revocation markers live only in Redis (a Redis flush silently un-revokes still-valid access tokens for up to 17 minutes).

**Second auditor.** Verified: CORS is an explicit three-origin list with credentials:false and bounded headers (app.ts:64-71), bearer-only auth removes CSRF, every response defaults to Cache-Control: no-store (requestContext.ts:13), the API sends X-Frame-Options SAMEORIGIN via helmet (PoC header check) and HSTS in production, and revocation markers are written for logout/reuse/admin revoke/deactivation (service.ts:64-88, users/index.ts) with the deactivation test green. The gaps are all confirmed: no Content-Security-Policy anywhere (helmet contentSecurityPolicy:false, Caddyfile sets only HSTS/nosniff/Referrer-Policy, apps/erp/nginx.conf sets none, apps/erp/index.html has no CSP meta, next.config.ts:35-45 has every header except CSP), no frame-ancestors/X-Frame-Options on the erp.* and my.* hosts so the staff SPA is clickjackable, the 7-day refresh token sits in localStorage on ERP (api.ts:5,12) and portal web (storage.web.ts:1-3), access-token revocation lives only in Redis (loadStaff never consults refresh_tokens, auth.ts:70-88) so a Redis flush un-revokes for up to 17 minutes, and refresh families never expire absolutely. One extra doc/config mismatch: .env.example:14 says origins are 'comma separated' but app.ts:65 passes each env string whole, so a comma-separated value silently breaks CORS (fail-closed, but a trap). Marginally lower than the first score because the clickjacking exposure of the ERP host is total, not partial.

**World-best would.** Ship a strict nonce-based CSP and frame-ancestors on every host, move web refresh tokens to httpOnly SameSite cookies with a CSRF token (or DPoP-bind them), persist revocation in Postgres with Redis as a cache, and add security-header assertions to the browser QA suite.

Evidence: `apps/api/src/app.ts:63-71 — @fastify/helmet (CSP off for the JSON API, HSTS in production) and CORS with an explicit three-origin list, credentials: false, bounded allowed/exposed headers; plugins/requestContext.ts:174 — Cache-Control: no-store default on every response`, `apps/api/src/plugins/auth.ts:41-49,72-73 and modules/auth/service.ts:63-88 — logout/reuse/admin revocation writes a Redis revoked:sid marker so unexpired access tokens die on the next request; sessions list/revoke-one/revoke-others for staff and customers (staff.ts:175-206, portal.ts:137-168)`, `apps/erp/src/lib/api.ts:5,12,205-216 and apps/portal/src/lib/storage.web.ts:1-3 — access token in memory but the 7-day refresh token lives in localStorage on the ERP and the portal web build (native uses SecureStore, storage.ts:4-5)`, `infra/caddy/Caddyfile:5-10,18-23 and apps/erp/nginx.conf:6-7 — erp.* and my.* hosts get only HSTS, nosniff (and Referrer-Policy on erp); no Content-Security-Policy, no X-Frame-Options/frame-ancestors; apps/erp/index.html has no CSP meta`, `apps/web/next.config.ts:35-45 — website sets nosniff, X-Frame-Options DENY, Referrer-Policy, Permissions-Policy, COOP and HSTS but no CSP`, `PoC: GET /api/v1/health headers → x-frame-options: SAMEORIGIN, content-security-policy: undefined, cache-control: no-store`

## Group E — Business Logic Correctness — **8500 / 10,000** (previous implementation: 6515)

| # | Factor | Previous (v1) | First score | Final score |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Flat generation algorithm | 685 | 860 | **850** |
| 2 | Pricing engine | 615 | 850 | **850** |
| 3 | Payment plan validation & tower assignment rules | 560 | 860 | **840** |
| 4 | Auto-dues engine | 650 | 870 | **850** |
| 5 | Ledger engine | 730 | 840 | **835** |
| 6 | Bucket cap rule & agreement revision | 660 | 870 | **865** |
| 7 | Cheque lifecycle, reversal, receipts | 675 | 860 | **855** |

| 9 | Booking atomic lock, cancellation & handover | 620 | 850 | **830** |
| 10 | Integrity report & repair | 630 | 860 | **850** |

### E1. Flat generation algorithm — 850/1000 (v1: 685)

**Strengths.** The algorithm is a pure, exhaustively tested domain function and the API only orchestrates it: every path (create, regenerate, structural edit, append floors) is one transaction, pricing goes through the same engine as the rate card, and the non-Available guard is enforced server-side before any delete. Flat-type changes re-snapshot exactly as the spec demands.

**Gaps.** The regenerate/structural-edit guard is a count check that locks only the block row (loadBlock FOR UPDATE), while POST /flats/:id/status locks only the flat row, so a HOLD committed between nonAvailableCount and `tx.delete(flats)` is silently deleted (bookings are protected by FK, holds are not). Regeneration deletes and recreates rows, so flat ids change and anything referencing a flat (documents, leads' interested flat) dangles. FLOOR_RISE uses floor − floorStart (pricing.ts:99) rather than the spec's (floor − 1); for ground-floor towers floor 1 pays one rise step, undocumented. Numbering patterns are not validated against the block type's has_floors flag (a villa block can be given a {floor} pattern).

**Second auditor.** Re-read packages/domain/src/flatGeneration.ts and apps/api/src/modules/inventory/blocks.ts: every claim checks out (unitFloors excludes skip/refuge/podium :127-157, pattern renderer :205-228 with INVALID_PATTERN guard, DUPLICATE_FLAT_NUMBER throw :309, generation inside the caller's tx :102-111, REGENERATE_BLOCKED :294-296, append-floors keeps only floors > old top :331) and inventory.test.ts ran 22/22 green in my own database. The regenerate guard is a count under a block row lock while POST /flats/:id/status locks only the flat (flats.ts:225), so a HOLD committed between nonAvailableCount and tx.delete is deleted; bookings are safe because bookings.flat_id references flats.id with no cascade (schema/bookings.ts:72-74), but any FK pointing at flats will make a regenerate fail with a misleading 400 from translatePgError (23503 → 'references a record that does not exist'). Regeneration always deletes and recreates ids. Numbering patterns are never checked against block_type.has_floors (has_floors only read for display, blocks.ts:39-55). FLOOR_RISE uses floor − floorStart, which the spec allows ('or as configured'). Slightly lower than the first auditor for the id-churn and the unguarded delete window.

**World-best would.** Take a block-wide lock shared with the flat status route (or SELECT every flat FOR UPDATE) before counting, diff-regenerate so unchanged positions keep their ids, and property-test numbering patterns against block types.

Evidence: `packages/domain/src/flatGeneration.ts:127-157 physicalFloors/unitFloors exclude skip, refuge and podium floors; :168-196 gap/overlap detection per unit floor`, `packages/domain/src/flatGeneration.ts:205-228 flatNumber renders {block}/{floor}/{pos}/{seq} with inline or :N padding; :269-338 generateFlats snapshots rooms/areas per flat, prices through pricingFn, throws DUPLICATE_FLAT_NUMBER instead of returning a partial list`, `apps/api/src/modules/inventory/blocks.ts:102-110 generateInto runs inside the caller's transaction; :148-166 POST /blocks creates block + stage snapshot + flats in one tx; service.ts:332-346 insertDrafts chunked by 400`, `apps/api/src/modules/inventory/blocks.ts:286-302 generate-flats returns 409 REGENERATE_BLOCKED when any flat is not AVAILABLE; :228-231 structural PATCH blocked the same way; :305-339 append floors generates only floors > old top`, `apps/api/src/modules/inventory/flats.ts:237-266 flat-type change re-snapshots rooms/areas (+ optional re-price) only while AVAILABLE`, `apps/api/test/acceptance/02-tower-generation.test.ts:117-130 (52 flats, skip 13, refuge 7); inventory.test.ts 22/22 green when run in this audit`

### E2. Pricing engine — 850/1000 (v1: 615)

**Strengths.** All four PLC modes, breakup lines and the Available-only re-price rule are implemented in a pure engine with integer-paise arithmetic, and activation/supersession is serialised both by a lock and a partial unique index. Per-flat audit rows give a price history.

**Gaps.** repriceFlats reads flat status without row locks and applyPriceUpdates (service.ts:391-399) has no `WHERE status = 'AVAILABLE'` guard, so a flat booked between the select and the update is re-priced after booking (money is safe because the booking snapshotted the price, but the flat row now contradicts the rule). FLOOR_RISE deviates from the spec's (floor − 1) when floorStart is 0. `effective_from` is informational only — nothing activates a card on its date. Draft-card PATCH replaces PLC rules wholesale with no semantic check that PERCENT values are percentages and paise values are paise beyond the schema.

**Second auditor.** Verified pricing.ts:72-108 (all four calc modes, PERCENT via basis points, FLOOR_RISE clamped ≥ 0), priceFlat breakup :140-159, resolveFlatPrice fallback :178-183, activation under a project row lock plus the rate_cards_one_active_uq partial index (rateCards.ts:276-288, schema/inventory.ts:340) and repriceFlats skipping non-AVAILABLE (service.ts:375). The first auditor's gaps are confirmed: applyPriceUpdates (service.ts:391-399) has no status predicate so a flat booked between the select and the bulk UPDATE is re-priced after booking (the booking's flat_snapshot keeps the agreed price so money is unaffected, but the flat row then violates the rule and bumps version); effective_from is only stored and sorted (rateCards.ts:41,129,150,256), nothing activates a card on its date. The FLOOR_RISE criticism is overstated because docs/SPEC.md:93 says '(floor − 1) … or as configured', and floor − floorStart equals floor − 1 for the normal floorStart = 1. Score unchanged.

**World-best would.** Make the re-price a version/status-conditioned UPDATE, schedule activation by effective date, and expose a per-flat price-delta preview before apply.

Evidence: `packages/domain/src/pricing.ts:72-108 plcApplies (ALL/FLOORS_GTE/CORNER/FACING_IN) and plcAmount (PER_SQFT, FIXED, PERCENT in basis points, FLOOR_RISE never negative), all integer-paise helpers`, `packages/domain/src/pricing.ts:140-159 priceFlat builds [label, amount] breakup + total; :178-183 resolveFlatPrice falls back to flat-type lump sum or rate × super built-up`, `apps/api/src/modules/inventory/rateCards.ts:266-298 activate supersedes the current ACTIVE card under a project row lock, backed by packages/db/src/schema/inventory.ts:340 partial unique rate_cards_one_active_uq`, `apps/api/src/modules/inventory/service.ts:363-388 repriceFlats skips non-AVAILABLE flats and types without a rate, writes per-flat audit rows; rateCards.ts:300-320 apply only for ACTIVE cards, dry_run supported`, `packages/domain/src/pricing.ts:213-251 bulkPriceEdit with optional floor-rise step; packages/domain/test/pricing.test.ts 9 tests green`, `apps/api/src/modules/inventory/service.ts:394-398 UPDATE flats … FROM jsonb_to_recordset WHERE f.id = x.id — no and f.status = 'AVAILABLE'`

### E3. Payment plan validation & tower assignment rules — 840/1000 (v1: 560)

**Strengths.** Validation is complete and pure (basis points, so 33.33+33.33+33.34 works and 99.99 is rejected), the tower-fit check names the missing stages per block as the spec asks, and the wizard dropdown is derived from the same applicability query the booking engine uses to reject non-applicable plans.

**Gaps.** "One default per scope" is only application-enforced (clearOtherDefaults then insert); there is no partial unique index on (project_id, block_id) WHERE is_default, so two concurrent POSTs can leave two defaults and resolveBookingPlan then picks one by name order. A project-wide assignment is validated only against blocks that exist at assignment time; a block created later (or whose stage sequence is edited while pending) can silently make an assigned plan unfit, and plan installment edits are not re-validated against towers the plan is already assigned to. Assignments cannot be audited for which tower a booking actually drew its plan from beyond planSnapshot.

**Second auditor.** validatePlan (paymentPlan.ts:107-176) is complete and exact (10000 bp, unique seq and normalised name, ≤1 ON_BOOKING/ON_POSSESSION, UNKNOWN_STAGE), assertPlanFitsScope names missing stages per block (plans.ts:93-108), applicableAssignments ranks block default > project default and flags exactly one default (:111-141), and money.test.ts 'payment plans ↔ towers' ran green. I confirm there is no partial unique index for is_default (schema/bookings.ts:39-41 only has the (plan, project, block) unique) so two concurrent POSTs can leave two defaults. More serious than the first auditor weighed: fit is checked only at assignment time — PATCH /blocks/:id and POST /blocks/:id/stage-sequence (blocks.ts:245-252, 266-283) replace a tower's stage sequence with no look at assigned plans, and plan edits in masters/kinds.ts:26-28 validate against the master stage list, not against towers the plan is assigned to. An ON_STAGE installment whose stage is absent from the tower is then simply never raised (triggerDate → null, dues.ts:126-129), the integrity report cannot flag it (evaluateDues sees an unsatisfied trigger), and completeBooking (bookings.ts:289-294) compares raised − paid, not agreed − paid, so a buyer can be handed over short of the agreed value. That drift path costs 20 points.

**World-best would.** Enforce the single default with a DB partial unique index, re-validate fit whenever a block's stage sequence or a plan's installments change (reject or flag), and version plan snapshots so bookings can show which plan revision they carry.

Evidence: `packages/domain/src/paymentPlan.ts:107-176 validatePlan: total must be exactly 10000 bp, unique seq, unique normalised names, ≤1 ON_BOOKING and ≤1 ON_POSSESSION, trigger details, UNKNOWN_STAGE via knownStageIds`, `apps/api/src/modules/masters/kinds.ts:362-372 validatePlan wired into payment-plan create/update`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/services/plans.ts:93-108 assertPlanFitsScope → 409 PLAN_STAGES_MISSING listing {id, name, block_id, block_name} per block; routes/plans.ts:84 on create and :117 re-checked on reactivation`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/services/plans.ts:111-141 applicableAssignments (block-specific + project-wide, effective dates, exactly one default flagged); :199-208 clearOtherDefaults; packages/db/src/schema/bookings.ts:39 unique (plan, project, block) nulls-not-distinct`, `apps/api/test/integration/money.test.ts:123-190 (3 tests) green in this audit; apps/api/test/acceptance/07-plan-assignments.test.ts`, `apps/api/src/modules/inventory/blocks.ts:266-283 stage-sequence replacement checks only assertStageSequence and allStagesPending; no assertPlanFitsScope`

### E4. Auto-dues engine — 850/1000 (v1: 650)

**Strengths.** The engine is exactly the spec's algorithm as a pure function, double-raising is impossible by construction (partial unique index + ON CONFLICT), locks are ordered to avoid deadlocks, and catch-up at booking, stage completion, daily job and admin run all go through one code path. The ₹ telescoping is property-tested.

**Gaps.** The spec's "at startup" run is not wired: server.ts/worker.ts only register cron schedules, so a server down across 00:30 IST misses time triggers until the next night or an admin run. nextNumber is consumed before the conflict-protected insert (dues.ts:73-85), so a race burns a DM- number. The name half of "seq OR name" idempotency is application-only (the index covers seq), acceptable only because validatePlan forbids duplicate names. raiseDuesForAll walks every ACTIVE booking serially each night with a full tower-stage preload but no batching or per-run marker.

**Second auditor.** Verified: installmentAmounts telescopes cumulative basis points (dues.ts:162-191, property-tested), hasDemandFor matches seq OR normalised name ignoring VOID (:194-197), isSatisfied treats ON_BOOKING as always satisfied (:237-242), raiseDues locks the booking before reading existing demands and inserts with ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING on demands_booking_seq_nonvoid_uq (services/dues.ts:56-85), lock order row → advisory (common.ts:77-80), cron 00:30 IST (jobs/index.ts:29), and money.test.ts (dues run racing payments; Flexi 40 % once under concurrent runs) passed 32/32 in my database. The 'burnt DM- number' gap is largely moot because the booking lock makes the conflict branch unreachable in practice. The missing startup run is confirmed: STARTUP_RECONCILIATION is only registered (admin/tools.ts:168) and server.ts/worker.ts only upsert the cron schedulers. New finding the first auditor missed: nextSequence (client.ts:138-151) is an upsert whose row lock is held to commit, and raiseDuesForBlock inside a stage completion (construction/service.ts:396) takes that demand-sequence lock after the first booking and then locks the next bookings; a concurrent 'run dues now' / daily run (one tx per booking: booking lock → nextNumber) locks in the opposite order, which is a textbook deadlock that Postgres resolves by aborting one side — and nothing in the API handles 40P01 or retries (no match for 40P01/deadlock in apps/api/src), so the loser is a 500. Rare, but real on a busy builder, hence −20.

**World-best would.** Enqueue an idempotent catch-up + reconciliation at boot, allocate the demand number after the insert wins, and partition the nightly run per project with a run marker so restarts resume rather than rescan.

Evidence: `packages/domain/src/dues.ts:162-191 installmentAmounts telescopes cumulative bp per bucket so Σ = agreed exactly; test/dues.test.ts:60-95 incl. a property test over random splits`, `packages/domain/src/dues.ts:194-197 hasDemandFor skips by seq OR normalised name ignoring VOID; :117-136 and :236-242 trigger dates/satisfaction for all five trigger types`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/services/dues.ts:54-101 raiseDues under lockBooking, ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING on packages/db/src/schema/money.ts:66 demands_booking_seq_nonvoid_uq, DEMAND ledger row, buyer + Accounts bells, audit`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/services/bookings.ts createBooking ("dues catch-up: raiseDues"), construction/service.ts updateConstructionStatus → raiseDuesForBlock in the same tx, jobs/index.ts:29 DAILY_DUES 00:30 IST, routes/dues.ts:18-31 POST /dues/run`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/common.ts:77-80 lock order row → advisory; money.test.ts:756-790 (dues run racing payments never double-raises; Flexi 40 % raised exactly once under concurrent runs) green in this audit`, `packages/db/src/client.ts:138-151 INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE on sequences inside the caller's tx — the (company, scope, fy) row stays locked until commit`

### E5. Ledger engine — 835/1000 (v1: 730)

**Strengths.** The worked example is reproduced at three levels (unit, API, seed), advances are negative dues exactly as specified, FIFO statuses are derived rather than stored, and the hot path is a single locked append with running balances rather than a rebuild.

**Gaps.** The two paths do not agree on order: appendEvent appends in posting order, but deriveLedger (used by every rebuild — agreement revision, undo, repair, recompute) sorts by ledgerEntryDate first, and payments carry entryDate = payment_date while demands carry the IST date of posted_at. A payment posted today but dated before an earlier-posted demand therefore gets a different seq and different intermediate running balances after any rebuild than it had before; totals agree so integrity finding (e) never notices, and the domain "derive vs append agree" test (ledger.test.ts:140-190) sorts events before appending so it does not exercise this. Cancellation refunds are only a closing row, not a money movement. writeSummary runs a correlated raised_percent subquery on every append.

**Second auditor.** The worked example is reproduced in the domain test (ledger.test.ts:60-77), via the API (money.test.ts:871-926, green here) and the seed self-asserts every row (seed/bookings.ts:107-136). applyEvent (ledger.ts:222-313) and withDerived make negative due = advance, demandStatuses allocates FIFO per bucket (:453-468), appendEvent is O(1) under the booking lock with seq = last + 1 (services/ledger.ts:91-101). I independently confirmed the ordering divergence: events.payment sets entryDate = payment_date while events.demand has no entryDate so ledgerEntryDate falls back to the IST date of posted_at (services/ledger.ts:128-136, domain ledger.ts:190-198); deriveLedger sorts by that date first (:355-368) whereas the live path appends in posting order, so after any rebuild (reviseAgreement :212, changePlan :247, undo, repair) a payment dated before an earlier-posted demand swaps seq and intermediate running balances; rebuild() only compares six aggregates, row count and final dues (:201-237) so finding (e) is blind to it, and the domain 'derive vs append agree' test pre-sorts events (test/ledger.test.ts:175). Customer statements therefore change their running-balance history after an agreement revision. Also confirmed: CANCELLATION is a closing row carrying −refund with no outflow record, and writeSummary runs a correlated raised_percent subquery per append (:83). −5 for the statement-history instability.

**World-best would.** Pick one canonical order (posting order with entry_date as a display attribute, or date order on both paths), have rebuild assert row-by-row equality with stored rows, and model refunds as first-class outflows.

Evidence: `packages/domain/src/ledger.ts:222-313 applyEvent for DEMAND/PAYMENT/REVERSAL/AGREEMENT_CHANGE/PLAN_CHANGE/other charges/CANCELLATION; withDerived makes due negative = advance; :339-344 appendLedgerRow O(1); :453-468 demandStatuses FIFO per bucket`, `packages/domain/test/ledger.test.ts:19-83 reproduces all 10 worked-example rows to the paise; apps/api/test/integration/money.test.ts:871-926 and acceptance/04-worked-ledger.test.ts do it through the API; apps/api/src/seed/bookings.ts:85-136 seed self-asserts every row`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/services/ledger.ts:91-101 appendEvent under lockBooking: seq = last + 1, running balances, booking summary columns written in the same unit of work`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/services/ledger.ts:157-199 sourceEvents rebuilds from demands/payments/charges/revisions with agreedAt(); :217-245 rebuild reports summary/row-count/final-due mismatches`, `packages/domain/src/ledger.ts:355-368 sortLedgerEvents orders by business date, then instant, then kind priority`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/services/ledger.ts:129 demand event {at: d.postedAt} (no entryDate) vs :135 payment event {entryDate: p.paymentDate}`

### E6. Bucket cap rule & agreement revision — 865/1000 (v1: 660)

**Strengths.** The cap is the only hard block and is applied in both places the spec names, revisions are stored as immutable before/after rows with a mandatory reason and permission, and the recompute is the same telescoping function the dues engine uses so raised figures stay to the paise.

**Gaps.** Posting counts only CLEARED money, so several pending cheques whose sum exceeds the headroom are all accepted and only fail one by one at clearing — spec-literal but there is no headroom-including-pending warning. The revision recomputes PAID demands too; a downward revision can shrink a demand below what FIFO already allocated and the issued demand letter becomes stale with no re-issue or notice. A revision with unchanged values is accepted (noise rows). No preview endpoint shows per-demand before/after before committing.

**Second auditor.** Verified bucketCapCheck returns the exact spec sentence (ledger.ts:413-433); assertBucketCap counts only CLEARED FLAT_VALUE money (payments.ts:23-28, 36-42) and is applied at posting (:97) and clearing (:145); reviseAgreement rejects per bucket below paid (BELOW_PAID, bookings.ts:189-190), stores an immutable before/after row with reason (:195), recomputes every non-void demand with the same telescoping demandAmountsFor the rebuild uses (:199-210), appends AGREEMENT_CHANGE with the raised adjustment and rebuilds (:211-213); route gated by agreement.edit and idempotent (routes/bookings.ts:229). money.test.ts 'BUCKET CAP at posting … after an audited agreement revision the same payment succeeds' passed here. Gaps confirmed: no no-op guard (nothing compares after with before), PAID demands are re-amounted too, pending instruments are ignored at posting so several pending cheques can exceed headroom and fail one by one at clearing (spec-literal), and no preview. Nothing material missed either way; −5 for the no-op/preview gaps weighing slightly more than the first auditor allowed.

**World-best would.** Offer a revision preview (per-demand deltas, affected letters), reject no-op revisions, and show headroom net of pending instruments at posting.

Evidence: `packages/domain/src/ledger.ts:413-433 bucketCapCheck returns the spec's exact message ("Account agreed …; already received …; cannot accept … – revise the agreement first")`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/services/payments.ts:36-42 assertBucketCap → 409 BUCKET_CAP_EXCEEDED at posting; :141 re-checked in clearPayment; :23-28 only CLEARED FLAT_VALUE payments count`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/services/bookings.ts reviseAgreement: 409 BELOW_PAID per bucket, revision row with before/after, telescoping recompute of every non-void demand via demandAmountsFor, AGREEMENT_CHANGE row carrying the raised adjustment, rebuild + recomputeDemandStatuses, Accounts + buyer bells`, `packages/contracts/src/modules/bookings.ts:234-246 reason mandatory and cash + account must equal the agreement value; routes/bookings.ts agreement-revisions gated by agreement.edit`, `apps/api/test/integration/money.test.ts:428-452, 471-509 and acceptance/05-bucket-cap-revision.test.ts (Sales 403, same payment succeeds after revision) green in this audit`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/services/payments.ts:23-28 clearedInBucket sums status = CLEARED and against = FLAT_VALUE only`

### E7. Cheque lifecycle, reversal, receipts — 855/1000 (v1: 675)

**Strengths.** Every state transition is a locked transaction with version checks, bounced and pending money never reaches paid/due, reversals are negative rows and cancelled receipts are kept, numbering is an atomic DB upsert per FY, and idempotency is enforced durably (Redis + Postgres) with mismatch detection.

**Gaps.** Reversing a payment on a COMPLETED booking is allowed and leaves the booking COMPLETED and the flat SOLD with due > 0 (docs/TODO-integration.md item 7, deferred). clearPayment stamps clearedAt = now and only audits body.cleared_on, so a cheque realised last week carries today's instant and the payment date as its business date rather than the realisation date. Idempotency scope is subject + key, so a different user replaying the same key re-executes. There is no FY close: a back-dated payment keeps consuming a prior FY's receipt sequence. Bounce charges are not modelled.

**Second auditor.** Verified postPayment (payments.ts:71-127): PENDING unless mode.clearsImmediately, pending_cheques_paise maintained, receipt allocated only after validation and inside the tx (so a rejected posting never burns a number — the upsert rolls back); clearPayment re-checks the cap and applies the ledger row (:140-157); bouncePayment sets BOUNCED + receiptCancelled with no ledger row and bells Accounts + buyer (:160-170); reversePayment appends a REVERSAL row (domain :247-255 negative amount) and cancels the receipt (:173-191); numbering is per (company, scope, FY) via nextNumber(…, body.payment_date) (numbering.ts:20-25); /payments is idempotent: 'required' (routes/payments.ts:38) with Redis lock + Postgres record and 422 on hash mismatch (idempotency.ts:60-110). Concurrency test (10 payments → contiguous receipts/seq) passed here. Confirmed gaps: reversal on a COMPLETED booking is allowed (:178 blocks only CANCELLED; TODO-integration #7), cleared_on is only audited and the ledger instant is now (:150-154), scope is subject + key (:75) so another user replaying the same key re-executes, no FY close. One cost not weighed by the first auditor: the receipt sequence row lock serialises every payment of the company for the full transaction (ledger append, audit, notifications), a throughput ceiling that is fine for staff-posted volumes but is a single hot row.

**World-best would.** Use the realisation date as the cheque's ledger date, add FY close/lock, model bounce charges as other-charge lines, and decide (block or reopen) reversal on completed bookings.

Evidence: `apps/api/src/modules/money/services/payments.ts postPayment: status PENDING unless mode.clearsImmediately, pendingChequesPaise maintained, receipt number allocated only after validation; clearPayment PENDING→CLEARED applies the ledger row; bouncePayment sets BOUNCED + receiptCancelled, no ledger row, Accounts + buyer bells`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/services/payments.ts reversePayment: CLEARED→REVERSED, REVERSAL row with negative amount (ledger.ts:247-255), receiptCancelled true, reason required; receiptData exposes subject_to_realisation and is_cancelled plus amountInWords`, `apps/api/src/lib/numbering.ts:20-25 + packages/db/src/client.ts:138-151 atomic per-(company, scope, FY) upsert sequence; packages/db/src/schema/money.ts:120 payments_company_receipt_uq; packages/db/src/sql/0001_triggers.sql:28-43 payments/demands never deleted`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/routes/payments.ts:38 idempotent: "required"; apps/api/src/plugins/idempotency.ts:66-97 400 IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REQUIRED, 422 IDEMPOTENCY_MISMATCH, replay with Idempotent-Replayed, Redis lock for concurrent duplicates`, `apps/api/test/integration/money.test.ts:360-470, 558-600 (10 concurrent payments → contiguous receipts and ledger seq) and acceptance/06 green in this audit`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/services/payments.ts:104-106 receipt number allocated after cap checks, inside the transaction`

### E8. Construction stage jump/undo rules — 875/1000 (v1: 690)

**Strengths.** Jump-ahead, the latest-only rule, the advance-does-not-block rule and notification retraction are all implemented exactly as specified and inside one transaction with the money engine; possession undo even reverses on-possession charges. Adversarial tests cover concurrent updates and double undo.

**Gaps.** Other-charge demand bells are keyed by entity `booking` (dues.ts:118), so a possession undo un-demands the charge but cannot withdraw its notification (TODO-integration item 6, deferred). The undo predicate is date-granular: a receipt dated the same day as the demand but posted before it still blocks. There is no server-side dry-run of a jump/undo (the "confirmation dialog listing intermediates" has to be assembled by the client). Notes on intermediate stages cannot be set in the same call.

**Second auditor.** Verified updateConstructionStatus (construction/service.ts:343-418): toComplete = every stage ≤ target not COMPLETED, intermediate dates validated (not future, ≤ completed_on, non-decreasing, reached stage must equal completed_on), raiseDuesForBlock in the same transaction with fresh tower stages, buyers notified only for stages with visible photos; undoStage (:457-499): 409 UNDO_NOT_LATEST via deriveProgress, voidDemandsForStage locks every affected booking then allocates CLEARED money FIFO and blocks when a funding receipt is dated ≥ demand_date (money/services/dues.ts:247-283), possession undo un-demands charges and blocks on paid ones, stage reset, withdraw(). construction.test.ts ran 20/20 green here. The first auditor's 'date-granular' criticism is actually spec-aligned (docs/SPEC.md:130 says 'dated on/after the demand'), so I do not count it. Confirmed gap: other-charge demand bells are keyed entityType 'booking' (dues.ts:118) so the possession undo cannot withdraw them (TODO #6). No server-side dry-run of a jump/undo. Also, the undo rebuilds ledgers through deriveLedger, inheriting the ordering divergence noted under the ledger factor. −5.

**World-best would.** Key every notification by the entity it describes, add a preview endpoint returning the stages, demands and buyers a jump/undo would touch, and compare instants rather than dates in the undo predicate.

Evidence: `apps/api/src/modules/construction/service.ts updateConstructionStatus: toComplete = every stage with sortOrder ≤ target not yet COMPLETED, intermediate dates validated (not future, ≤ completed_on, non-decreasing along the sequence), raiseDuesForBlock in the same transaction, buyers notified only for stages with buyer-visible photos`, `apps/api/src/modules/construction/service.ts undoStage: 409 UNDO_NOT_LATEST via deriveProgress().latestCompleted, possession other charges un-demanded (paid ones block), stage reset to PENDING, % recomputed, withdraw(STAGE_ENTITY_TYPE)`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/services/dues.ts:247-283 voidDemandsForStage locks every affected booking, demandFunding allocates CLEARED payments FIFO by payment date, blocks when a funding receipt is dated ≥ demand_date, voids + withdraws demand bells + rebuilds ledgers`, `apps/api/src/modules/notifications/service.ts:49-53 withdraw deletes unread and flags read notifications`, `apps/api/test/integration/construction.test.ts 20/20 green in this audit (jump, undo, UNDO_BLOCKED, possession undo, concurrent version conflict); acceptance/09`, `docs/SPEC.md:130 UNDO RULE 'paid by a payment dated on/after the demand'; apps/api/src/modules/money/services/dues.ts:266 f.date >= d.demandDate`

### E9. Booking atomic lock, cancellation & handover — 830/1000 (v1: 620)

**Strengths.** The flat lock is a real row lock plus a DB partial unique index, so two concurrent bookings can never both succeed; cancellation and handover enforce every spec condition and explain all failures at once; Booked/Sold are unreachable outside the engine.

**Gaps.** The flat flip is an unconditional UPDATE after the check rather than a conditional `UPDATE … WHERE status IN (…) RETURNING` — correct under the row lock but not the guarded form the spec describes. The agreement value is never compared with the flat's snapshotted price nor gated by a permission (spec: "editable with permission"), so any booking.create user can book a ₹1 Cr flat at ₹1. Cancellation records the refund only in a JSON column and a closing row — no refund outflow/receipt, so Roznamcha and collections cannot show money paid back. Expired holds (hold_until) are neither auto-released by a job nor treated as bookable by others.

**Second auditor.** Verified createBooking (bookings.ts:77-165): SELECT … FOR UPDATE on the flat, AVAILABLE or own HOLD (settings.manage override), parking slots locked and checked, flat → BOOKED, catch-up raiseDues, unique violation mapped to 409; bookings_one_active_per_flat_uq (schema/bookings.ts:114); manual BOOKED/SOLD rejected (flats.ts:101-102); cancelBooking and completeBooking enforce every listed condition (:255-302); 'two simultaneous bookings → exactly one succeeds' passed here. The first auditor's gaps are confirmed and one is worse than scored: the API never compares agreement_value_paise with the flat's snapshotted price and gates nothing behind a permission (contracts/bookings.ts:65-75 only checks split = total and > 0; route needs booking.create), while the ERP enforces 'editable with permission' purely client-side (wizard/StepAgreement.tsx:72 readOnly without agreement.edit) — a Sales user can book a ₹1 Cr flat at ₹1 through the API with no audit of the deviation. hold_until is never consulted (createBooking :88-89 treats any HOLD by another user as unbookable; no sweep job), so expired holds freeze inventory until someone with settings.manage releases them. Refunds live only in the cancellation JSON. completeBooking compares raised − paid rather than agreed − paid (:289-294), which is spec-literal but makes the plan-fit drift a handover hole. −20.

**World-best would.** Use the conditional UPDATE as the lock, gate price deviation behind a permission with a reason, model refunds as outflow records with their own numbering, and sweep expired holds.

Evidence: `apps/api/src/modules/money/services/bookings.ts createBooking: flat SELECT … FOR UPDATE, status must be AVAILABLE or own HOLD (settings.manage may override), parking slots locked and checked, flat → BOOKED, catch-up raiseDues; unique violation mapped to 409 FLAT_NOT_AVAILABLE`, `packages/db/src/schema/bookings.ts:114 partial unique bookings_one_active_per_flat_uq as the last line of defence; apps/api/test/acceptance/03-concurrent-booking.test.ts (2 and 5 simultaneous attempts → exactly one 201)`, `apps/api/src/modules/inventory/service.ts:27-30 and flats.ts:100-114 reject manual BOOKED/SOLD (422 ENGINE_STATUS); inventory.test.ts green in this audit`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/services/bookings.ts cancelBooking: pending cheques block, refund ≤ paid, refund + deduction ≤ paid, open demands → CANCELLED, flat and parking → AVAILABLE, CANCELLATION closes the ledger; postPayment/reversePayment reject non-ACTIVE/cancelled bookings`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/services/bookings.ts completeBooking: possession COMPLETED, total due ≤ 0, other charges due ≤ 0, no pending cheques → COMPLETED and flat SOLD, listing every problem in 409 HANDOVER_BLOCKED`, `apps/erp/src/features/bookings/wizard/StepAgreement.tsx:72,85 'The agreement value needs the Edit agreement permission to change' — client-side only; apps/api/src/modules/money/routes/bookings.ts:139 POST /bookings permission booking.create`

### E10. Integrity report & repair — 850/1000 (v1: 630)

**Strengths.** All five finding types are produced from the same engines that raise dues and build ledgers, repair is conservative (never touches demands with money applied), runs are persisted with counts, and nightly findings reach the roles the spec names. Manufactured anomalies are exercised end-to-end.

**Gaps.** "Admin-confirmed" is only a permission: the repair body has no explicit confirm flag or dry-run diff, and repair silently re-runs the report rather than acting on the run the admin reviewed. Finding (e) compares aggregate figures, row count and final due only, so row-level drift (ordering/running balances, see the ledger factor) is invisible. CANCELLED ledgers are skipped entirely and COMPLETED ones only get c/d/e, so tampering there is never reported. Each run rebuilds every booking's ledger serially (O(bookings × events)) with no incremental mode, and STARTUP_RECONCILIATION is registered but never enqueued at boot.

**Second auditor.** Verified integrity.ts:49-94 emits UNMAPPED, DUPLICATE, UNSATISFIED (ACTIVE only), MISSING via evaluateDues (ACTIVE only) and SUMMARY_MISMATCH from rebuild(); repair (:124-151) voids only paid_paise = 0 demands, raises missing, rebuilds and recomputes in one tx per booking with audit rows; nightlyIntegrity notifies Super Admin + Accounts (:154-167); routes under admin.tools (routes/dues.ts:33-74); acceptance 09 tampers a summary and sees SUMMARY_MISMATCH; money.test.ts integrity case passed here. Confirmed gaps: repair re-runs the report (persist: false) and uses run_id only to derive booking_ids (routes/dues.ts:50-55) — it acts on the live state, not the reviewed findings, and there is no explicit confirm/dry-run; finding (e) compares six aggregates + row count + final dues only, so the row-order drift from the ledger factor is invisible; CANCELLED bookings are excluded (:39-46) and COMPLETED ones skip a/b; every run rebuilds every booking serially; STARTUP_RECONCILIATION is registered but never enqueued at boot. Nothing additional missed in either direction; −10 because 'admin-confirmed' is weaker than the spec intends.

**World-best would.** Require run_id + an explicit confirmation that pins the reviewed findings, compare a row-level ledger hash, scan incrementally (changed-since) with parallel workers, and enqueue reconciliation at startup.

Evidence: `apps/api/src/modules/money/services/integrity.ts:49-94 checkBooking emits UNSATISFIED_DEMAND (a), MISSING_DEMAND via evaluateDues (b), DUPLICATE_DEMAND (c), UNMAPPED_DEMAND (d) and SUMMARY_MISMATCH from rebuild() mismatches (e)`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/services/integrity.ts:124-151 repair voids only demands with paid_paise = 0 (others listed in skipped_with_payments), raises missing ones, rebuilds and recomputes under one transaction per booking, audits each`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/services/integrity.ts:154-167 nightlyIntegrity notifies Super Admin + Accounts; apps/api/src/jobs/index.ts:30 NIGHTLY_INTEGRITY 00:45 IST; runs persisted in integrity_runs`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/routes/dues.ts:33-74 GET /dues/integrity, POST /dues/integrity/repair (by run_id or booking_ids), GET /dues/integrity/runs under admin.tools`, `apps/api/test/integration/money.test.ts:733-755, 791-818 and acceptance/09-stage-jump-undo-integrity.test.ts:214 (tampered summary + unsatisfied demand flagged and repaired); seed asserts zero findings`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/routes/dues.ts:50-55 run_id → booking_ids filter only; repair() calls runReport(db, scope, { persist: false }) afresh (integrity.ts:125)`

## Group F — API Design & Developer Experience — **7365 / 10,000** (previous implementation: 4715)

| # | Factor | Previous (v1) | First score | Final score |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | REST design consistency & naming | 670 | 815 | **805** |
| 2 | OpenAPI accuracy & documentation | 420 | 735 | **720** |
| 3 | Idempotency semantics | 470 | 835 | **810** |
| 4 | Optimistic concurrency semantics | 560 | 790 | **770** |
| 5 | List capabilities: filtering/sorting/search/CSV everywhere, allow-listed sort fields, strict query params | 610 | 830 | **800** |
| 6 | Error codes machine-readability & consistency | 650 | 720 | **705** |
| 7 | API versioning & backward compatibility strategy | 490 | 610 | **600** |
| 8 | Testing: unit vs integration coverage, independence from live server/seed | 380 | 830 | **815** |
| 9 | CI/CD, linting, formatting, type-checking | 165 | 600 | **570** |
| 10 | Developer onboarding | 300 | 780 | **770** |

### F1. REST design consistency & naming — 805/1000 (v1: 670)

**Strengths.** One registration helper gives every one of the 464 operations the same validation, serialisation, guard and tag; naming is uniformly kebab-case with nested sub-resources and verb-free GETs; the error envelope, list envelope, 201-on-create and 404-for-out-of-scope rules are written down in docs/API-CONVENTIONS.md and enforced by integration tests.

**Gaps.** DELETE status codes are split 15×204 / 3×200 and PUT /booking-drafts is a non-idempotent upsert that creates rows without an id in the URL. Seventeen list-shaped GETs skip the standard envelope (bare arrays on the portal/public surfaces, items-only on auth/sessions, booking-drafts, presets, co-applicants) so clients need two list codepaths. ETag is emitted on every response even with Cache-Control: no-store, and single-operation tags (files, demands, dashboard) fragment the surface in Swagger. Nothing enforces these conventions at registration time beyond the guard check.

**Second auditor.** Re-verified by an in-process OpenAPI dump (361 paths / 464 operations, registry 471) and by reading apps/api/src/lib/routes.ts: the single route() helper really does enforce exactly-one-guard, strict query objects and auto-tags, and conventions.test.ts (7 tests) passed on my own kkt_audit_F2 database. The first auditor's gaps hold: PUT /booking-drafts (money/routes/bookings.ts:80-92) inserts a new row whenever body.id is missing or not owned, so a retried PUT silently creates duplicates; 6 GETs return bare arrays and 11 return items-only objects. Two of the cited gaps are weaker than framed: the three DELETE→200 routes (auth/staff.ts:196, auth/portal.ts:158) return a meaningful {revoked:n} body, and most non-envelope GETs are small fixed sets (categories, assignees, sessions) where no pagination is sensible. New observation the first auditor missed: GET /api/v1/openapi.json and /docs are public (system/index.ts:66, routes.ts PUBLIC_PREFIXES), so the full staff/admin route inventory with schemas is disclosed to anonymous callers — a deliberate but debatable design choice for a builder's ERP. Net: slightly lower for the non-idempotent PUT and the inconsistent portal/public list shapes, which are the surfaces third-party consumers actually hit.

**World-best would.** Encode the conventions in route(): one list envelope for every collection (array responses only via an explicit opt-out), DELETE → 204 always, PUT only on fully-identified resources, and a registry test that fails CI on any new deviation; publish the surface as a stable resource model document generated from the registry.

Evidence: `apps/api/src/lib/routes.ts:87-113 — single route() helper: Zod body/query/params, 2xx response map, exactly one of permission|audience|public, auto tags from the first path segment`, `In-process OpenAPI/registry dump (buildApp + app.swagger()): 471 registry entries (GET 231, POST 174, PATCH 46, DELETE 18, PUT 2), 396 staff / 35 portal / 40 public; URLs are kebab-case nouns with action sub-resources (/payments/:id/clear|bounce|reverse, /bookings/:id/other-charges/:lineId/demand)`, `apps/api/src/modules/money/routes/bookings.ts:139-143 and :229-233 — creates answer 201 with the created row; 53 POST ops are 201, 121 state-change POSTs are 200 with the updated row (OpenAPI dump)`, `OpenAPI dump: DELETE /auth/sessions, /portal/auth/sessions and /notices/:id answer 200 while the other 15 DELETEs answer 204; PUT /booking-drafts (bookings.ts:80-92) upserts and inserts a new row when no id is given`, `packages/contracts/src/common.ts:212-220 listEnvelope {items,total,page,page_size,next_cursor} used by 51 list schemas, but 17 GETs return bare arrays or items-only shapes (portal/bookings, public/projects, auth/sessions, booking-drafts, site-visits/today …)`, `apps/api/test/integration/conventions.test.ts:46-69 — envelope keys, X-Request-Id, ETag/304 and Cache-Control asserted (passed when I ran it against kkt_audit_F)`

### F2. OpenAPI accuracy & documentation — 720/1000 (v1: 420)

**Strengths.** The spec is generated from the same Zod schemas that validate requests and compile responses, so it cannot drift from behaviour, and an integration test proves every registered route is in it; request and response bodies are fully typed, list filters appear as typed query parameters, and two security schemes separate staff and portal tokens.

**Gaps.** The document is a route inventory rather than consumer documentation: no operationIds and zero named component schemas mean generated clients get anonymous, duplicated types; no 4xx responses, no error-envelope schema, no header parameters for the idempotency/version contract and no examples, so the most important semantics (409 VERSION_CONFLICT details, 422 IDEMPOTENCY_MISMATCH, Idempotent-Replayed) are invisible in Swagger. Five binary endpoints are typed as any instead of declaring application/pdf or image/* content, and ten request sub-schemas collapse to {} (free-form records). The staff surface has no human-readable map beyond Swagger.

**Second auditor.** My own in-process dump confirms every statistic the first auditor gave: 464 operations, 0 without a summary, 0 operationIds, 0 named component schemas, 0 operations documenting any 4xx/5xx response, 0 header parameters (Idempotency-Key, If-Match, If-None-Match absent), 0 examples. system.test.ts:24-39 does prove route coverage, and because the document is produced by fastify-type-provider-zod from the validating schemas it cannot drift. But the semantics that matter most to a client — the error envelope, 409 VERSION_CONFLICT/422 IDEMPOTENCY_MISMATCH payloads, Idempotent-Replayed, Retry-After — are entirely invisible in the spec, and with no components a generated client gets hundreds of anonymous duplicated types. docs/API-CONVENTIONS.md 'OpenAPI' section exists but there is no generated api_map.md and no typed client package. I lower slightly because the document is accurate but is not usable as consumer documentation for an integrator, and the most important contract (errors/headers) lives only in prose.

**World-best would.** Register contracts schemas as named components with operationIds, add the shared error responses (400/401/403/404/409/422/429) and the Idempotency-Key/If-Match/If-None-Match headers to every relevant operation, declare binary content types, and commit a generated api_map.md plus a typed client package built from the spec in CI.

Evidence: `apps/api/src/app.ts:85-94 — @fastify/swagger (OpenAPI 3.1) with fastify-type-provider-zod jsonSchemaTransform; UI at /api/v1/docs (app.ts:94); document at /api/v1/openapi.json (modules/system/index.ts:66-70)`, `Generated document (my in-process dump): 361 paths / 464 operations, every operation has a summary and a 2xx JSON schema (0 missing), Zod preprocess fields render correctly (public enquiry phone → pattern ^[6-9]\d{9}$), typed query params for list filters (GET /customers: 17 typed params)`, `apps/api/test/integration/system.test.ts:24-39 — asserts every registry route appears in the OpenAPI document with its method`, `Dump statistics: operationId 0 of 464, components.schemas 0 (every schema inlined), header parameters 0 (Idempotency-Key, If-Match, If-None-Match undocumented), examples 0, description on 3 operations, only 2xx responses documented (464/464) — the error envelope and 4xx/409 codes never appear in the spec`, `Untyped bodies: z.any() on 5 streaming routes (documents/routes.ts:204, portal/index.ts:68, web/publicContent.ts:571, web/publicForms.ts:193, admin/backup.ts:423) and 10 {} sub-schemas in request bodies (e.g. POST /customers/:id/portal/enable body, reports/presets params)`, `No docs/generated/api_map.md exists; the hand-written docs/API-CONVENTIONS.md Appendix A/B/C fixes auth, public and portal surfaces but the 396 staff routes are documented only through Swagger`

### F3. Idempotency semantics — 810/1000 (v1: 470)
